## Status Legend

- [ ] Not started
- [~] Partial / needs rework
- [x] Complete (pruned to the reference list at the end of this file)

**ID conventions:** `P<tier>.<n>` — items filed on this plan. `D<n>.<n>` — items migrated from the retired `DOCS_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (2026-08-08 merge), codes preserved for git-history traceability; new items always take the next free P-code. Tier 5 is the docs queue: `/next-docs` selects from Tier 5 only, every other tier belongs to `/next-items`.

## Architecture Migration (cross-reference)

> **2026-06-11:** A full architecture review produced a target architecture (regime-as-data rulepacks, producer-sealed eager stage edges, uniform stage model) and a phased (0–8) strangler migration plan: **`docs/plans/target-architecture-migration.md`**. Phases 0 and 1 landed 2026-06-11 (merged via PR #386); Phase 2 (dead-path deletion, branch-path error channel, protocol diet, sentinel-to-None) landed 2026-06-12 on `feat/phase2`. Next: Phase 3 (producer-sealed edge contracts). Plan-curator: schedule migration work per phase from that document rather than minting parallel P-codes; phase-relevant existing items are called out in its "folds in" notes (e.g. P2.21 → Phase 4; P1.183/P2.16b → Phase 4 aggregate-stage conversion; P8.39/P8.53/P5.17 CCR wirings → Phase 4 "ordinary registered stage" work; P2.5/P2.7/P2.25/P2.27/P2.28/P4.20 → Phase 7 per-template modules). The do-not-do register in that plan is binding for all batches (notably: no bulk golden regeneration; no agent edits to `tests/oracle/`; Float/String nulls never filled to 0.0).

**WS7 — SA institution / corporate risk weights** (files: `risk_weights.py`, `crr.py`, `common.py`, `schemas.py`)
- **P1.314** [x] **FIXED 2026-08-15** (`02a54fc4`, batch 20260815-1) — the Art. 114/115 "and funded" limb now applies on the direct path. **The bullet was wrong twice and the premise audit caught both.** (1) It named `is_domestic_currency` as the edit site, but that expression **also** feeds `apply_sovereign_floor_for_institutions` (`risk_weights.py:385`), whose PS1/26 Art. 121(6)(a) reads *"is not in the local currency"* with **no funding limb** — tightening it there arms a floor on rows the rule does not reach (measured 0.40 → 1.50, a 3.75× overstatement). (2) It missed **Art. 115(5)/(2) entirely**, which carry the same verbatim limb and share the same defective expression. Fix is TWO derived expressions routed per-consumer — `is_domestic_currency_funded` → the two CGCB 0% branches, `is_uk_domestic_funded` → the four RGLA branches — with `with_funding_limb` in `engine/eu_sovereign.py`; the sovereign floor and `classify/approach.py` deliberately keep the UNFUNDED expression, each with a comment saying why. Null convention **PERMISSIVE**: `funding_currency` is `required=False` on three schemas, and a strict reading would strip 0% from every gilt in every dataset that does not populate it. **Direction RWA-INCREASING, measured not argued** — a 69,120-row grid per regime through the pre-fix and post-fix engines gives `moved=1032, INCREASED=1032, DECREASED=0` in all four cells, with the 0%-rung fall-through confirmed *exercised* so the result is not vacuous. 13 legs; four are green-on-both-sides guards and **all four were proven live by mutation** (composite-into-RGLA → leg J; strict-null → leg C; funded-flag-into-the-floor → legs I/K; `pl.col("currency")` vs the pre-FX expression → the E2E survivor). Also removed the misleading `"denominated (and funded)"` comments that documented a limb the code did not implement. Residuals filed: **P5.53** (Tier 2 is structurally blind — no registered reporting portfolio sets `funding_currency`), **P1.356** (CRR Art. 119(2)/(3) carry the same limb, unimplemented), **P1.357** (`approach.py`'s `is_eu_domestic_sovereign` is dead code in both regimes). · *Original bullet:* (MED) **Art. 114(4)/(7) domestic-currency 0% ignores the FUNDING limb — an UNDERSTATEMENT.** Verified verbatim: CRR Art. 114(4) reads "Exposures to the central government of the United Kingdom and the Bank **denominated and funded in sterling** shall be assigned a risk weight of 0%" (`crr.pdf` p.112); Art. 114(7) likewise "denominated **and funded** in the domestic currency"; PS1/26 Art. 114(4) is identical (`ps126app1.pdf` p.35). The engine's gate is `is_uk_domestic = (cp_country_code == "GB") & (ccy_expr == "GBP")` plus the EU sibling — **denomination only**. So a UK/EU sovereign exposure denominated in the domestic currency but **funded** in another currency takes 0% where Art. 114(1)/(2) is due. **Internal inconsistency, the P1.253 pattern:** the guarantee-substitution path DOES apply the funding limb (`engine/eu_sovereign.py::build_domestic_cgcb_guarantor_expr`, used at `sa/rw_adjustments.py:676`), so direct and guarantor legs disagree on the same rule. Inputs already exist — `funding_currency` is on `data/schemas.py` and sealed on the `crm_exit` edge. **Care needed on the null convention:** `funding_currency_expr` is null-permissive (falls back to denomination), which is right for P1.229 but here would preserve today's over-relief; tightening it is the actual decision. Found while closing P1.284. **Understatement, higher severity than either item it came from. Effort: M**

**WS8 — SA other classes (CIU, defaulted, ECAI seniority/currency)** (files: `risk_weights.py`, `equity/calculator.py`, `enrich.py`, `ratings.py`, `schemas.py`)
- **P1.315** [ ] (LOW) **Art. 127(4) commercial limb of the P1.257 blend, deliberately deferred.** The Art. 127(4) flat-100% applies to the part secured "in accordance with **Article 126**", and the engine's only proxy for the Art. 126(2) qualifying test is `has_income_cover` — which **P1.263 records as carrying the INVERTED sense on the CRR branch** (the 50% CRE weight is granted when `has_income_cover=True`, the opposite of Art. 126(2)(b)). Because the blend REDUCES RWA, building the commercial limb on a flag known to be backwards would grant relief to the wrong population; gating on the CRE class alone would be broader than the article allows, also in the relieving direction. **Blocked on P1.263** — settle the flag's meaning first, then mirror the RRE blend with the Art. 126(2)(d) 50% cap (`COMMERCIAL_RE_PARAMS["ltv_threshold"]`). **Effort: S**
- **P1.259** [ ] **RE-SCOPED 2026-07-26** (batch 20260724b) — **investigated, NOT implemented; the bullet as written must not be executed.** (MED) PS1/26 Art. 138(1)(g)/139(6): no field flags a rating as incorporating implicit government support, so support-inflated bank ratings are accepted; from 1 Jan 2027 they must be excluded for institution obligors. **Effort: M→L** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 138(1)(g)/139(6) · Ev: `data/schemas.py` RATINGS_SCHEMA, `engine/stages/hierarchy/ratings.py`
  - **Regulatory claim VERIFIED** against `ps126app1.pdf` p.72: Art. 139(6) reads "An institution, when determining the risk weight of an exposure to an issue where: (a) **the obligor is an institution**; and (b) there is no issue-specific credit assessment available … that does not incorporate assumptions of implicit government support …, **shall use the higher of** … (ii) … **disregarding point (g) of Article 138(1)**." Mandatory ("shall"), institution-obligor-only, B31-only (CRR Art. 138 has (a)-(f); CRR Art. 139 has (1)-(4)).
  - **DO NOT ship the literal P2.40a→P2.40b schema-first split.** `contracts/edges.py` is *not* needed for a schema-only add (`RAW_TABLE_EDGES` is derived from the schema dicts, so it does not force single-stream) — but that is exactly the problem: the hierarchy stage selects an explicit column list and `conform` strips undeclared columns, so a schema-only field would be **unreachable**, not merely unread. That is strictly worse than P1.291's dead `is_solicited` / `rating_is_inferred`. **Hit rate on speculative rating flags in this repo is 1 of 3** — only `rating_is_issue_specific` ever got a consumer.
  - **TWO fields, at different grains, and one cannot substitute for the other.** `rating_incorporates_government_support` on RATINGS_SCHEMA (per assessment — genuinely varies by agency: Moody's BCA vs support-uplifted LT, S&P SACP vs ICR), nullable Bool with **no default** so the tri-state survives `edge_columns_from_specs`' Boolean fill; and `is_government_owned_or_sponsored` on COUNTERPARTY_SCHEMA (per counterparty, invariant across agencies), default `False` — an affirmative carve-out, absence cannot manufacture one. A single collapsed "usable" flag is **regulatorily insufficient**: Art. 139(6) requires computing candidate (ii) *from the assessment (g) disallows*, which a pre-collapsed flag cannot reconstruct.
  - **Default must be null, not True.** `default=True` would drop every rated institution exposure in every fixture and every live book to SCRA on the day the field lands. Null = "no ECAI methodology review performed" is a **governance** gap and belongs in the error channel (a DQ warning on an institution obligor under a B31 pack), not in the risk weight. Repo precedent: `is_equivalent_jurisdiction` and `is_lease_collateral_attested` both carry "no default on purpose".
  - **BLOCKER FOUND WHILE SCOPING (not in the investigation):** the consuming filter must be pack-`Feature`-gated, but **no resolved pack is threaded into this stage at all** — `ratings.py::build_rating_inheritance_lazy`, `graph.py::build_counterparty_lookup` and `resolver.py::_build_rating_inheritance_lazy` all lack a `pack` parameter. Gating therefore requires signature changes across three hierarchy modules on the highest-blast-radius path in the engine. This is the main reason the item was not landed in batch 20260724b.
  - **Recommended cut — at the REGULATORY seam, not the schema/logic one.** **P1.259 (revised):** both fields + the **Art. 138(1)(g) suppression limb only**, behind a b31-only Feature (`sa_implicit_support_ratings_disapplied`), implemented as a filter in `ratings.py` **before** the Art. 138 ranking — order matters, because 138(1)(g) removes assessments from the *selection set*, which changes which row the second-best rule picks; annotating the winner is wrong. Because suppression is pre-selection, `external_cqs` simply resolves to the clean rating or null, so **no new column crosses the hierarchy edge and `contracts/edges.py` stays untouched**. Number-neutral on the existing book (no fixture sets the flag). Plus the pack threading above.
  - **P1.259b (new, Effort L, SINGLE-STREAM):** the Art. 139(6) higher-of. Needs a second CQS carrier for candidate (ii), an aggregate for "does a clean issue-specific assessment exist" computed over the pre-selection set, and edits to four edge contracts (`CP_LOOKUP_EDGE`, `CP_LOOKUP_PARENTS_EDGE`, `crm_exit`).
  - **Scope extension the bullet misses:** under CRM substitution the exposure is weighted as an exposure to the *guarantor*, so where the guarantor is an institution 138(1)(g) bites on its rating too. `crm/guarantees.py` derives `guarantor_cqs` from the same Art. 138-resolved `cqs`, so a suppression inside `ratings.py` propagates automatically — correct, but it means guarantee-substitution numbers move as well and must be tested deliberately.
  - **Sequencing:** **P1.291 is the policy item and should run first or be folded in** — it decides "consume or delete" for the existing dead rating flags, which sets the rule for whether P1.259 may split at all. P1.261 (`rating_currency_scope`) is a third addition to the same RATINGS_SCHEMA block and will textually conflict, so keep all three in one stream. Note `docs/specifications/basel31/sa-risk-weights.md:909-926` publishes a "Not Yet Implemented — Input-Schema Gap" warning telling firms to pre-adjust `external_cqs` offline; a schema-only landing would not let that be withdrawn.

Under CRR a subordinated exposure to a CQS1 corporate gets 20% not the unrated 100%. Null the CQS for subordinated rows where the table RW would be lower. CRR. **Understatement. Whoever takes P1.288b must confirm the IRB side or explicitly scope it out.

- **P1.289** [x] **FIXED 2026-08-13** (`9dcb1643`) — Art. 134(7) requires `t = max(1, nearest whole years REMAINING)`; the engine divided by the raw fraction. Both directions wrong, worst case **+32.9pp understatement** at 1.49 years left. The remaining-term basis and the floor at 1 were already correct. Ties round **down** (2.5y → t=2 → 50%): at exactly one half neither whole year is "nearest" and the article is silent, so the tie resolves toward the higher weight; `ceil(t - 0.5)` is used rather than a rounding routine because banker's rounding sends 2.5 → 2 but 3.5 → 4, conservative on one and not the other. **Regime symmetry CHECKED, not assumed** — PS1/26 Art. 134(7) is word-for-word identical with a note that it corresponds to CRR Art. 134, unlike Art. 120 where the two genuinely diverge. `test_residual_lease_two_point_five_years` pinned the defect (asserted `1/2.5 = 40%`) and is inverted. Ratchets improved. Residual filed: P1.352 (zero rows in 26 registered runs). · *Original bullet:* (LOW) Art. 134(7) — leased-asset residual RWEA uses fractional `1/t`, not nearest-whole-year `t`; both-direction misstatement. Round to nearest whole year before the division. **Effort: S** | Ref: Art. 134(7) · Ev: `risk_weights.py:1186-1187,1358-1359` · §5 WS8
- **P1.290** [x] **CLOSED 2026-08-15 as a DOCUMENTED SCOPE EXCLUSION** (batch 20260815-1) — **the bullet's own "or a validation error" branch is UNBUILDABLE, and building it would have been worse than nothing.** Confirmed by absence, not inferred: `rg -ni 'nth.to.default|n.th.default|basket' src/rwa_calc/` returns **zero hits**; `VALID_ENTITY_TYPES` has no such member; `GUARANTEE_SCHEMA` (`schemas.py:1180-1242`) models protection **received** only — `guarantor` + `beneficiary_reference` into the firm's own book, with **no direction flag, no basket linkage, no `n`, and no protection-nominal field**; and the CCR estate's nearest columns are different products the article does not govern (`asset_class="credit" & ~is_long & is_index` is a sold **index** CDS, proportional not nth-to-default; `cdo_attachment`/`cdo_detachment` is a **CDO tranche**, a securitisation position). A guard on those would fire on input the engine handles correctly — the P1.354 false-positive shape, and LESSONS B1's "guard on a condition no input produces". **PS1/26 Art. 134(6) SURVIVES** — positively identified, not assumed: Article 134 closes with `[Note: This rule corresponds to Article 134 of CRR…]` while Art. 135(2) `[Note: Provision left blank]` and Art. 136 `[Note: Provision not in PRA Rulebook]` were visible in the **same extraction**, so both discriminating forms were seen. Text is word-for-word CRR bar "will"→"shall". **So no pack `Feature` is needed and this is a gap in BOTH regimes.** **The bullet cited 1 of 3 controlling articles** — an exclusion naming only Art. 134(6) leaves an IRB firm silently uncovered: **CRR Art. 153(8)** carries the same aggregation with a different cap (EL×12.5 + RWEA ≤ nominal×12.5), **PS1/26 Art. 153(8)** adds a limb CRR lacks (*"(c) A 1,250% risk weight shall apply to positions in a basket for which an institution cannot determine the risk weight under the IRB Approach"*), and **CRR Art. 147(10) / PS1/26 Art. 147(9)** assigns the sold-protection exposure to the basket members' class. **Highest-value line of the item:** `docs/specifications/crr/sa-risk-weights.md:1422` was the repo's ONLY description of Art. 134(6) and it was **wrong** — *"Per Art. 266-270"*, a cross-reference the article does not contain (it prescribes the aggregation in-line). Corrected and marked NOT IMPLEMENTED, with the verbatim text, the three articles, why no DQ error is possible, and the direction. Also recorded at `data/schemas.py` (following the securitisation-exclusion precedent) and in `tests/unit/test_other_items_risk_weights.py`, whose docstring enumerated Art. 134 as (1),(2),(3),(4),(7) and **silently omitted (5) and (6)** — that silence is what made the gap invisible. **Direction: anti-conservative** — a first-to-default basket of five unrated corporates on 10m nominal is 500% × 10m = **50m** RWEA against 10m contrived or 0 omitted; implementing it is RWA-increasing and needs a basket-membership table plus the IRB sibling and classification limb, i.e. a P1.288a/b-shaped schema-enablement programme, **not "Effort: S"**. **Ev pointer was stale** and is corrected for the record: `risk_weights.py:1175-1189,1347-1361` is the QCCP / subordinated-debt / ECA-MEIP area, not Other Items, which has drifted to `:1299-1312` (B31) and `:1499-1513` (CRR). · *Original bullet:* (LOW) Art. 134(6) — no representation of sold nth-to-default basket protection; implement input + aggregation or document the exclusion + a validation error. **Effort: S**
- **P1.291** [x] **FIXED 2026-08-13 — consumed as a WARNING, and BOTH prescriptions in the bullet were wrong.** The bullet offered "consume the flag (config switch + DQ warning) **or remove the dead column**". Verbatim CRR/PS1-26 Art. 138 chapeau (`crr.pdf` PAGE_INDEX 134): *"An institution **shall use solicited credit assessments. However it may use unsolicited credit assessments if the competent authority has confirmed** that unsolicited credit assessments of an ECAI do not differ in quality from solicited credit assessments of this ECAI."* **The permission is per-ECAI and supervisor-granted** — not a property of the rating row, and carried by no input. So: **filtering** on `is_solicited` alone would suppress every unsolicited assessment and deny ratings a firm is entitled to use wherever the confirmation exists (wrong in the DENYING direction), and **deleting** the column would remove the only carrier for a genuine provision — the flag is not regulatorily dead, only unread. New **DQ015** (`unsolicited_rating_not_filtered_warning`) surfaces the condition and leaves the decision with the firm, which is what the error channel is for. **One warning per run, not per row**: which ECAI confirmations a firm holds is a portfolio-level governance fact, and per-row emission would bury it on exactly the book that uses an unsolicited ECAI systematically. A **null** flag stays silent — nullable with a `True` default means "not stated", and warning on null would be the same false-positive shape P1.354 had just fixed on the neighbouring DQ006. Pinned by `tests/unit/test_p1_291_unsolicited_rating_warning.py` (8 tests) including a parametrised guard that **the rating is still used** whatever the flag says, so the warning cannot silently become a filter. `engine_eager_collect_sites` bumped 49→50 with justification: the error message carries a count, so it needs a materialisation, exactly as the sibling `_record_misscoped_st_ratings` does; the presence guard was removed rather than banked, keeping `engine_presence_guard_sites` flat. **This unblocks P1.259**, which sequences behind it as the consume-or-delete policy decision — the answer is CONSUME. Full suite 11,867 passed. · *Original bullet:* (LOW) CRR/PS1-26 Art. 138(2) — `is_solicited` / `rating_is_inferred` are declared but read nowhere; unsolicited ratings are used unfiltered. The cap was applied unconditionally, sitting just OUTSIDE the `if is_b31:` block above it, so a CRR full-risk commitment to issue a low-risk item fell **100% → 0%**, taking the whole exposure value with it. ⚠ **CITATION TRAP, now recorded at the site:** **PS1/26** Art. 111(1)(c) IS the lower-of rule; **CRR** Art. 111(1)(c) is "20 % if it is a medium/low-risk item" — same address, unrelated provisions, so a CRR citation of 111(1)(c) is not authority for this cap. Gated on the existing `firb_uses_sa_ccf` pack **Feature** (the carrier the Table A1 overrides already use), so no pack entry was added and no generated page restaged — which is why this was landable where P1.296 and P1.267 are not. **TWO defect-pinning tests inverted** (`TestCommitmentToIssueLowerOf::test_crr_sa_commitment_mr_to_issue_lr_uses_lr_ccf` 0.0 → **0.5**, `::test_firb_crr_commitment_mr_to_issue_lr_uses_lr` 0.0 → **0.75**) — the seventh instance of that pattern this batch, and both were named after the defect. The F-IRB value is 0.75 not 0.50 because under CRR the F-IRB CCF ladder is its own (Art. 166); it is PS1/26 Art. 166C(1) that equates it to the SA CCF, which is why the two carriers agree under B31 and diverge here. Pinned by `tests/unit/test_p1_265_lower_of_ccf_is_b31_only.py` (4 tests) on the FR-over-LR maximum-swing pair, with a B31 survives-the-change guard and a never-raises property so a `max_horizontal` slip is caught. Gate the cap on the B31 feature or document it as an opt-in B31 election. CRR. **Effort: S** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 111(1)(c) · Ev: `ccf.py:524-546` · §5 WS10
- _P1.266 (WS10, spec-stale): stale 'Not yet implemented' P2.31/32/33 block in `credit-conversion-factors.md` — **actionable bullet in Tier 5.**_
- **P1.267** [ ] **RESCOPED 2026-08-13 (LOW→MED) — ⚠ DO NOT HARMONISE THE TWO ARMS. The CRR half is understated as the bullet says; the B31 half is REFUTED, and a shared 100% fallback would be wrong under PS1/26 in two places, in opposite directions.**
  - **THE STRUCTURAL ARGUMENT THE BULLET MISSES, and it is stronger than "be conservative".** CRR Annex I (`crr.pdf` PAGE_INDEX 433-435) gives all four categories an "other items" residual, but they are **not equivalent**: 1(k) is *"other items also carrying **full risk**"* — unconditional — while 2(b)(iv), 3(b)(ii) and 4(c) each read *"other items also carrying … risk **and as communicated to [the competent authority]**"*. **Only the full-risk residual is available without notification.** An item the engine cannot classify has by definition not been notified as medium risk, so it **cannot lawfully sit in the 50% bucket via 2(b)(iv)**. The residual must be 100% as a matter of the text, not of prudence.
  - **PS1/26 TABLE A1 IS STRUCTURALLY DIFFERENT** (`ps126app1.pdf` PAGE_INDEX 29-31), despite its `[Note: Column A of Table A1 corresponds to Annex I of CRR]`. It has **three** residual limbs and **none carries a notification condition**: Row 1(f) *"any other issued off-balance sheet items that **have** the character of credit substitutes"* → 100%; **Row 3** *"Other issued off-balance sheet items that do **not** have the character of credit substitutes"* → **50%**; **Row 5** *"**Any other commitment** not subject to a conversion factor of 10%, 50% or 100%"* → **40%**. So under B31 an unclassifiable **issued** item is 50% **by the text of Row 3 — the engine's value is RIGHT, not a fallback** — and an unclassifiable **commitment** is **40%**, where the engine's 50% **OVERSTATES**. Opposite direction to the bullet. This is the Art. 120 trap (two regimes, genuinely different texts, one shared implementation) and it is live here.
  - **The 50% is deliberate, not dictionary ordering** — the pack comment claims it "catches unrecognised `risk_type` values under **both** CRR Art. 111 and PRA PS1/26 Table A1". The defect is that it was *reasoned* as regime-invariant when the texts are not. The regime maps already diverge correctly elsewhere (measured `_SA_CCF_CRR_MAP` OC 0.50 / LR 0.00 vs `_SA_CCF_B31_MAP` OC **0.40** / LR **0.10**); only the residual is wrongly shared, and it lives in `common.py`, which is where the assumption is encoded.
  - **REACHABILITY — the bullet's "malformed input only" is WRONG, but the exposure is bounded.** `_validate_table_columns_batched` filters `is_not_null()` **before** the domain test, so a **null** `risk_type` is schema-valid (`required=False`), raises nothing, and silently takes the residual. That is well-formed input. But the residual only bites when `nominal_amount ≠ 0`, and across all 26 registered runs (386 rows) **0 rows** meet both conditions — fourth consecutive item this batch with zero registered reachability. Two things that look like findings and are **not**, both measured: `CCR_DERIVATIVE`/`CCR_SFT` do reach `apply_ccf` but carry `nominal_amount = 0` so `_nominal_is_zero` forces `ccf = 0.0` (their EAD comes from SA-CCR), and the estate's 344 null-`risk_type` rows are on-balance-sheet with nominal 0.
  - **MEASURED, per 1,000,000 of OBS nominal on a row that bites:** CRR any-unresolved 0.50 → **1.00** (+500,000 EAD, **+50pp understatement**); B31 unresolved **issued** 0.50 → **0.50** (**already correct**); B31 unresolved **commitment** 0.50 → **0.40** (−100,000 EAD, **−10pp — the engine overstates, and fixing it is RWA-REDUCING** and feeds the B31 output floor directly, so it needs its own sign-off line).
  - **THE ERROR-CHANNEL HALF IS THE HIGHER-VALUE ONE and the bullet under-rates it.** DQ006 already fires for non-null unrecognised strings; what produces **no signal at all** is a **null** `risk_type` on a row with non-zero nominal. That is the change that actually helps a preparer. Per CLAUDE.md it must accumulate into `list[CalculationError]`, never raise, and needs a code allocation.
  - **TWO DEFECT-PINNING TESTS BLOCK IT** — `tests/unit/test_ccf.py::test_null_defaults_to_mr` (:1435) and `::test_unknown_defaults_to_mr` (:1440), whose docstrings state "should default to MR (50%)". C1 shape (explicit wrong expectation), the fifth instance of the pattern this batch. The other 166 tests in that file use recognised codes and are unaffected.
  - **IMPLEMENTATION — no new pack entries needed; every target value is already in the regime maps.** CRR arm `.otherwise(table["FR"])`; B31 arm split on the existing `is_obs_commitment` column (already ensured with a default and already read by `_firb_ccf_for_col`) — commitment → `table["OC"]` (0.40), issued → `table["MR_ISSUED"]` (0.50). Then **delete `sa_ccf_default` from `packs/common.py`**, since a shared entry encodes the refuted assumption and `ccf.py:207` is its only consumer.

Plus ladder controls and a `nominal_amount=0` control, and an error-channel assertion that a facility with a real undrawn limit and a null `risk_type` yields exactly one new DQ error where today it yields zero. **Effort: S-M** | Ref: CRR Annex I 1(k)/2(b)(iv); PS1/26 Table A1 Rows 1(f)/3/5 · Ev: `packs/common.py::sa_ccf_default`, `engine/ccf.py:207`

- **P1.268** [x] **REFUTED 2026-08-15 — ALREADY IMPLEMENTED IN FULL, and the bullet's prescribed fix would have been a REGRESSION.** Commit `dfe9c399` (2026-07-21) shipped the entire Art. 113(6) path: a `core_uk_group` entity-registry column, an `intragroup_entity_reference` tag on four exposure schemas, scope-resolver eligibility, an `intragroup_zero_rw` Feature + `intragroup_zero_rw_pct` ScalarParam in **both** packs, a sealed edge column (`edges.py:663-674`), the final-RW override `apply_intragroup_zero_rw` (`engine/sa/rw_adjustments.py:637`), propagation to synthetic undrawn rows, a registered `multi_entity_cug` fixture, and **32 tests across four files**. ⚠ **The bullet prescribes "an opt-in flag + config gate" — that is strictly WORSE than what exists and would re-open a bypass the shipped code deliberately closes.** The implemented design is the per-row **attested-input** shape (matching `model_permissions`, not a config boolean) with three gates that must all hold: an attested per-counterparty perimeter (the firm declares the permission as a *counterparty set*, and `_cug_eligibility` requires the reporting entity itself to be in it, so both ends are attested); a run-shape gate (individual-basis only); and an **anti-bypass clobber** — `_set_cug_eligibility` (`resolver.py:379`) **overwrites** `intragroup_zero_rw_eligible` on every lending row, True only where the tag matches an attested member, so a stray user-supplied `True` can never win the 0%. **The `Ev:` pointer is a trap twice over**, and would have caused a re-confirmation: `enums.py:764-767` is the **IRB Art. 150 permanent-partial-use** enum, unrelated to SA weighting — and its neighbour's docstring (`enums.py:772`) cites *"Art. 113(7) (IPS)"*, an **omitted** paragraph, which is almost certainly what seeded the bullet's "113(6)-(7)" framing. Meanwhile `risk_weights.py` genuinely has no 0% branch because the override lives in `rw_adjustments.py` — **so an agent re-checking only the bullet's own cited files would falsely re-confirm the bullet.** **UK CRR Art. 113(7) is OMITTED** (`crr.pdf` p.idx 111: `F242 7 . . .`, omitted 31.12.2020 by the EU Exit Regulations), so there is no UK IPS 0% rule to implement; the §5 audit entry's "IPS members under 113(7)" is EU CRR. PS1/26 *does* have a 113(7) but it is a **continuing-compliance obligation** on the permission holder, not a risk-weight rule. Reachability confirmed end-to-end: `tests/integration/test_multi_entity_cug_zero_rw.py` (10 tests) runs the full pipeline against the `multi_entity_cug` fixture. - **P1.296** [~] **RESCOPED + IMPLEMENTED AND REVERTED 2026-08-13 — ⚠ BLOCKED ONLY BY A SANDBOX RESTRICTION, not by anything in the code.** The change is written, verified and green on 11,860 tests; it cannot be committed because completing it requires regenerating two files under `.claude/skills/`, which is **read-only in this environment**. Make that directory writable and this is a ten-minute replay.
  - **THE DIAGNOSIS AND DIRECTION ARE CORRECT.** Art. 222(3) verbatim (`crr.pdf` p.216): the collateralised portion takes *"the risk weight that they would assign under Chapter 2 … **where the lending institution had a direct exposure to the collateral instrument**"*, floored at 20%. So the weight is **derived, not prescribed** — whatever Chapter 2 gives a direct holding. CRR Art. 133(2) → 100%; PS1/26 Art. 133 → **250%** (pack `equity_sa_risk_weights`, `LISTED`/`EXCHANGE_TRADED` = 2.50). Verified per-regime resolution after the move: crr 1.00, b31 2.50.

Unlike P1.305 — where the file's recorded reasoning was right and the bullet wrong — here the file is the error. An implementer reading that comment concludes the item is refuted.
  - **250% FLAT — the bullet's "transitional 160% in 2027" is WRONG.** PRA Rules 4.2/4.3 are a **firm-specific grandfathering provision for firms coming off IRB equity permission**, carrying an irrevocable opt-out, applied as an upward `max_horizontal` floor on the dedicated `equity_exposures` path (`engine/equity/calculator.py:560-566`; corroborated by the P1.317 review). Art. 222(3) asks what **Chapter 2** would assign, and an IRB-legacy override is not a Chapter 2 weight.
  - **Direction: RWA-INCREASING, monotonic, NO sign reversal** — only the collateral leg of `share × collateral_RW + (1 − share) × obligor_RW` moves, so the blend rises at every share and every obligor weight. The P1.330 two-directional caution does not bite here.
  - ⚠ **SEQUENCE WITH P1.346.** Post-fix the blend exceeds the *unsecured* weight for any obligor below 250%: a CQS-1 institution at 20% with full main-index-equity cover goes **20% → 250%**, a 12.5× increase **caused by posting collateral**. That is Art. 222(3) working as written (it carries a 20% floor and no cap, and Art. 222(1) makes the method an election), but it is the P1.346 uncapped-blend shape at roughly **ten times** its current magnitude. Landing P1.296 alone makes P1.346 far more visible in output.
  - **Population is main-index equity only** — P1.330 (`bfcc8597`) gated the Simple Method on Art. 197(1)(f) alone, so non-main-index equity is ineligible before the weight is assigned. `simple_method.py:156` still tests `["equity","equity_main_index","equity_other"]`, wider than the eligibility gate; harmless because those rows never arrive, worth a comment not a change.

A fixture leg with an `equity_main_index` pledge is the real deliverable, not the scalar.

#### Tier 1 — follow-ups raised by the 2026-07-24 plan drain (batch plan-drain-2026-07-24)

> Registered by the orchestrator while draining P1.248/249/251/252/253/276/277/278/279/281. Each was found by an implementer or a reviewer, deliberately scoped out of its parent item, and is recorded here rather than lost. Severity in brackets; where a fix would REDUCE RWA it is flagged, because those need arbitration rather than drive-by implementation.
>
> **Process finding worth acting on first (Effort: S, high leverage):** four separate pre-existing tests/fixtures in that batch pinned a premise the audit had refuted, and every one was detectable from the words **"uniform"** or **"all classes"** in a test name or docstring (e.g. `test_crr_uniform_floor_all_classes_equal`, which asserted the very thing P1.277 removes). Two of the four passed *anyway* because their assertions were relative rather than absolute, so they silently protected the defect. **Grep `tests/` for those two phrases and audit each hit against its article.**
>
> **Extended 2026-08-13 (P1.333 test wave) — add `"backward compat"` to the grep list, and add a second, harder shape.** P1.333's single defect-pinning test was `TestBackwardCompatibility::test_missing_columns_backward_compat`, asserting an unfloored 40% on a row the Art. 121(6) floor should have raised to 100%. **The class name was doing the concealing:** "backward compatibility" frames an unfloored weight as a deliberate legacy accommodation, so a reader sees an intentional carve-out rather than a capital shortfall, and the accompanying prose ("only a frame that omits the column entirely … is left unfloored") asserted an absent-column asymmetry that measurement then refuted — `ensure_columns` fills both columns, and the 40% was pure P1.333. Renamed to `TestNullAndMissingSovereignInputs`. **The harder shape, found in the same sweep and not caught by any phrase grep:** `test_uk_institution_usd_floor_applies` has a docstring asserting "FX, sovereign floor applies" while the floor was in fact abstaining — the 40% assertion survived either way because a CQS 1 jurisdiction's floor is 0% and cannot bind. That is a test whose **docstring claims a behaviour its assertion is structurally incapable of detecting**, which no keyword search finds. The generalisable check is to ask, of any test naming a rule, *what value would this assertion take if the rule never ran at all* — if the answer is "the same", the test does not cover the rule it names.

- **P1.297** [ ] (LOW) CRR Art. 147(3)(c) — an `international_organisation` IRB row takes the 0.03% corporate PD-floor arm although Art. 147(3)(c) assigns 0%-risk-weight IOs to the central-government class, which P1.277 left unfloored. Same for the sovereign-treated guarantor labels (named MDB, `rgla_sovereign`, `pse_sovereign`), whose SA-class label does not distinguish them from institution-treated siblings. **Conservative (over-floors), so fixing it REDUCES RWA — needs arbitration.** Note the repo's Art. 118 IO list is separately wrong (P1.283), so widening on that label would compound a known defect. **Effort: S** | Ev: `engine/irb/formulas.py::_pd_floor_expression` ladder
- **P1.298** [ ] (MED) Art. 116(5) guarantor limb — a non-equivalent third-country PSE *guarantor* still confers a 20%/50% substituted weight; P1.252 gated only the direct path. Needs a guarantor-side equivalence input threaded through the guarantee join (`gtr_`-prefixed, mirroring `cp_is_equivalent_jurisdiction`); a partial UK-only mirror would be WORSE than none, since it would deny relief to genuinely equivalent jurisdictions. Limb (b) does not arise — the guarantor PSE branch has no short-term treatment. Fold in the audit's DQ warning (a firm in an equivalent jurisdiction that has not populated the new column takes a silent 5× RWA increase — a real data-migration hazard). **P1.253 is the precedent for why the guarantor path must not be assumed to follow the direct one. Effort: M**
- **P1.299** [x] **REFUTED 2026-08-13 — the prescribed fix is RWA-REDUCING and regulatorily prohibited. Do not re-file.** The bullet reads the org-wide path's omission of `has_modelled_lgd` as a defect against the model-permission path. **Measured end-to-end through `PipelineOrchestrator`, it is the reverse: the org-wide path is the conservative one, and the model path carries the real defect (filed as P1.348).**
  - **UNSECURED — label-only, prices identically to the decimal.** GB corporate, EAD 1,000,000, internal PD 2%, `lgd` null: org-wide `advanced_irb` with LGD 0.45 gives `rwa_final` **1,150,026.3541**; the F-IRB counterfactual gives **1,150,026.3541**. B31: **851,295.1048** both ways at LGD 0.40. So on the unsecured shape the only consequence is a mis-stated `approach_applied` reaching COREP C 08.01/08.02 and Pillar 3 CR6 — real, but not "number-affecting" as filed.
  - **COLLATERALISED — the prescribed fix REDUCES capital 60%.** Same row plus 600,000 eligible cash: today `advanced_irb`, `lgd_input` 0.45, `rwa_final` **1,150,026.35**; post-"fix" `foundation_irb`, `lgd_input` 0.18, **460,010.54** — **−690,015.81, −60.0%** — because F-IRB reads `lgd_post_crm` while A-IRB reads `lgd`. The bullet is label-only exactly where it is harmless and RWA-reducing exactly where it bites.
  - **THE FIX IS PROHIBITED BY ARTICLE.** CRR Art. 149(2) verbatim (`crr.pdf` p.144): an institution with Art. 151(9) own-estimate permission *"**shall not revert** to the use of LGD values … referred to in Article 151(8) unless … not proposed in order to reduce the own funds requirement … and … prior permission of the competent authority."* The prescribed change **is** that reversion, and it is measured to reduce own funds by 60%. PS1/26 Art. 150(1) rule para 4(b) says the same in words: F-IRB must not be used *"in order to achieve lower capital requirements, compared to using the Advanced IRB Approach."*
  - **A-IRB WITH NO OWN LGD ESTIMATE IS AN AUTHORISED STATE, not a contradiction.** PS1/26 Art. 151(7)(b) verbatim (`ps126app1.pdf` p.98): an A-IRB institution *"shall … provide its own estimates of LGDs … **except where the institution uses LGD values in accordance with Article 161(2) or points (b) or (c) of Article 164(1)**"* — and Art. 164(1)(b) prescribes a flat 100% A-IRB LGD for purchased-receivable dilution risk. Art. 143/151(9) grant permission at **class** level; they do not condition per-row routing on a populated field. So the null is a **data-quality breach of Art. 151(7)(b)**, not an approach reassignment — and the engine already treats it that way: BR001 fires today (`irb_lgd_branch_reason == UNKNOWN_FALLBACK`, WARNING), and `contracts/validation.py:1385-1420` names "the A-IRB rows carrying no LGD from any source" as the defect that instrument exists to expose.
  - **IT WOULD ALSO BREAK P1.278.** The Art. 160(2) top-down purchased-receivables population has **no own LGD by construction** — that absence is the article's own trigger — and today correctly reaches A-IRB with the Art. 161(1)(f)/(g) supervisory LGD. Under the prescribed predicate an A-IRB-only permission map drops it to Standardised entirely. The six assertions at `tests/unit/classifier/test_p1_278_top_down_pd.py:305,323-324,382-384,400-401,463-464,481-482` are **not** oversights: the docstring at `:289-296` documents the org-wide no-LGD-requirement behaviour deliberately, written by the P1.278 author who considered the tighter route and rejected it.
  - **`has_modelled_lgd` is not a permission predicate at all** — it is `pl.col("lgd").is_not_null() | pl.col("ccr_modelled_lgd").is_not_null()` (`stages/classify/approach.py:114`), a raw data-presence check standing in for a permission. Do not treat the model path as the reference implementation merely because it differs.
  - **Unreachable from the public API in any case.** `CreditRiskCalc._create_config` (`api/service.py:273-300`) passes only `permission_mode`; `"standardised"` → `sa_only()` (False everywhere), `"irb"` → the all-`False` early exit at `permissions.py:349-353` or the model path. Org-wide IRB requires constructing `CalculationConfig(irb_permissions=…)` directly. No `RUNS` portfolio and no golden uses it. **Effort: 0 — closed.**
- **P1.348** [ ] **RESCOPED 2026-08-13 (audited, then implementation ATTEMPTED AND REVERTED — read this block before the original text below).** The core premise survives: an A-IRB-permitted row with a null LGD really is demoted, and under an A-IRB-only permission map it really does reach `standardised`. **But three of the bullet's four load-bearing claims are wrong, and the fix it prescribes is the wrong fix.**
  - **The 60-65% headline is a wrong-baseline artifact.** It compares an F-IRB result *with* collateral relief against an A-IRB result *without* it. Like-for-like, the understatement is **400,000.00 vs 460,158.33 = 13.1%**, not 60-65%. Still a real understatement; an order of magnitude smaller, and the item's priority should be set from 13.1%.
  - **The article is wrong.** The SA fall-through is governed by **Art. 149(1)** (IRB → SA), not Art. 149(2), which governs the A-IRB → F-IRB reversion. The bullet's whole "prohibited without prior competent-authority permission" framing is attached to the wrong limb.
  - **The prescribed fix is wrong, and its blast radius is understated.** "Remove the conjunct here" moves **~30 golden rows** across `rich`, `irb-classes` **and `crm-substitution`** — the third portfolio the bullet does not mention. The correct fix is narrower: keep `has_modelled_lgd` on `airb_expr`, and route only `model_airb_permitted & ~has_modelled_lgd & ~model_firb_permitted` to F-IRB instead of letting it fall to SA.
  - **Attempted, measured, reverted.** The audit measured the narrow fix at **zero** moved rows across the 7 registered portfolios × both regimes — but that measurement is **scoped to registered reporting portfolios and does not cover acceptance fixtures**, where it is *not* zero. Applying it produced **2 failures + 18 errors**:
    - `tests/unit/test_classifier.py::TestModelPermissions::test_airb_permission_without_lgd_falls_to_sa` — `assert 'foundation_irb' == 'standardised'`. **A test that pins the defect in its own name** (8th instance of this pattern in this batch). It must be inverted, not satisfied.

- **P5.57** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-15, trap found by the P1.310 audit) — `OWNER: P5.57`. A fragment with EMPTY `entries` auto-absorbs divergent pack entries, so routine work silently restages a read-only skill file.** `scripts/generate_regulatory_tables.py` has exactly one such fragment — `.claude/skills/basel31/references/what-changed.md` / `regime-divergence` — and `_render_fragment_body` falls back to `_divergent_names(packs)` for it. That region therefore **auto-absorbs every simple-shape entry present in both packs whose values differ**. Consequence: adding any new CRR-off/B31-on `Feature` — a routine pattern in this repo — restages a file that is **read-only in some environments**, blocking the item with no obvious cause. (A non-divergent, identical Feature is safe.) This has now blocked or part-blocked five items: P1.296, P1.267, P1.334, P1.302, P1.310. **Fix options, in order:** (a) make the fragment's membership explicit rather than computed, so a new Feature does not silently join it; (b) have `--check` report *which entry* newly entered a fragment, so the cause is visible; (c) make `.claude/skills/` writable in the sandbox. **Effort: S for (b), M for (a)** | Ref: `scripts/generate_regulatory_tables.py::_render_fragment_body`, `_divergent_names`

- **P1.320** [x] **FIXED 2026-08-15** (`8ec7d302`, batch 20260815-1) — the QRRE obligor aggregate now counts each facility limit **once**. **The bullet understated the mechanism**: the multiplier is not ×2 but × the number of exposure legs, and the `_UNDRAWN` leg is one instance of it, not the mechanism — a GBP 30,000 card across four drawn loan rows measured GBP 120,000 and demoted with **no undrawn leg at all**. Proven mechanically at audit: the bullet's 70k/20k card measures **140,000**, tipping point bisected to 45,000.50. The carrier (`facility_limit`) and partition (`counterparty_reference`) were both right — the **GRAIN** was wrong. Deduplicates on `(counterparty_reference, parent_facility_reference)`, the functional determinant of the summed value. **Direction RWA-REDUCING** (−38.4% at LGD 0.60, PD 3%; sign-flipping to +2.6% below the B31 QRRE 50% LGD floor), shipped on output-floor invariance measured against the **real pre-fix engine** rather than a monkeypatch: `s_trea` 59,718,958.33, `sa_rwa_total` 21,001,125.00, `floor_threshold` 1,759,825.00 and total `ead_final` 78,396,500.00 all byte-identical, only `u_trea` moving — and **not vacuously**, since ~46,500 of EAD genuinely changes class meanwhile. **Seven wrong implementations were run and all seven bite**, and the two guard legs partition the failure space: leg (vi) (mixed candidate/non-candidate group) catches the ordinal variants including the `pl.len()` shape the repo's own `classify/attributes.py` sibling suggests; `TestNullParentRowsAreNotDeduplicated` catches the pooling ones. Three constraints are load-bearing and each is commented: `cum_sum` of the candidate **mask** not a row count; the `.over()` calls **inlined inside** `partition_by_nullable(...)` so the AST contract gate sees them guarded (no allowlist entry — 14 of 14 `rich`-portfolio rows carry a null parent); and `facility_limit` as the third argument so null-parent rows are not pooled. D1/D1b `known_disagreement` entries retired (`c8c2cb23`). Residuals filed: **P1.355** (the root/sub grain over-count survives — drawn legs key on the sub, waterfall legs on the root), **P5.54** (Tier 2 cannot see this change). · *Original bullet:* (MED — number-affecting) **QRRE per-individual aggregate double-counts a split revolving facility, so partially-drawn cards are denied the Art. 154(4) QRRE treatment.** The per-individual QRRE test sums `facility_limit` over the obligor's candidate rows; a revolving facility with undrawn headroom is split by the hierarchy stage into a drawn child and a synthetic `_UNDRAWN` leg, and **both** carry the parent's `facility_limit`, so one GBP 70,000 card is measured at GBP 140,000 and tips over the QRRE aggregate ceiling. Consequence: essentially every partially-drawn credit card is classified `retail_other` not `retail_qrre`, loses the Art. 154(4) flat 0.04 correlation (the retail curve is used instead), and disappears from the COREP C 08.xx and Pillar 3 CR6 QRRE rows. Only the fully-drawn shape is in the reporting fixture, which is why it never surfaced (`.claude/LESSONS.md` B5). Register: `classification_table.toml` D1 (`exposure_class_irb`) + D1b (`exposure_class`, the routing carrier) — both stand or fall together. **Effort: M** | Ref: CRR Art. 154(4)(c); PS1/26 Art. 147(5A)(c) · Ev: hierarchy split-facility `facility_limit` propagation; per-individual QRRE aggregate
- **P1.321** [ ] (MED — RWA-REDUCING, needs arbitration) **The B31 large-corporate F-IRB test keys on the literal `cp_entity_type == "corporate"`, so `company` and `specialised_lending` obligors escape it and receive A-IRB where F-IRB is due.** A GBP 600m-revenue obligor typed `company` receives A-IRB where the identical obligor typed `corporate` correctly receives F-IRB; the same escape applies to the unknown-revenue conservative default. Direction: **RWA-REDUCING**, because A-IRB's own LGD is normally below the Art. 161(1)(aa) 40% / (a) 45% supervisory values — so fixing it INCREASES capital and needs sign-off rather than drive-by. The fix pattern already exists in the repo: the ADC corporate gate in `attributes.py` tests `["corporate", "company", "specialised_lending"]` and the class map sends all three to CORPORATE. - **P1.322** [ ] (MED — approach routing) **`covered_bond` and `high_risk` are emitted as `exposure_class_irb` values, which are not Art. 147(2) IRB classes, so an IRB-permissioned firm's holdings are forced Standardised.** CRR Art. 147(2) / PS1/26 Art. 147(2) enumerate the IRB classes and neither admits a covered-bond or high-risk class; the residual limb (CRR Art. 147(7) / PS1/26 Art. 147(4A)) assigns any such credit obligation to the **corporate** class. Blocked on regulatory sourcing, not engineering. **Effort: M (sourcing) + S (pin)** | Ref: CRR Art. 501 (Part Ten) · Ev: `tests/oracle/ORACLE_DERIVATIONS.md` ORC-060, ORC-097

#### Tier 1 — follow-ups raised by batch 20260808-1624 (P1.316 / P1.318 / P1.319)

> Five defects surfaced by the Wave-0/Wave-1 adversarial gate that were **not on the plan**. Three were found while auditing a bullet that turned out to be wrong about something else. Direction is flagged on each; the RWA-reducing ones need arbitration, not drive-by implementation.

- **P1.323b** [~] **SAME DEFECT AS P1.333 — close together, and read P1.333's REFUTATION before attempting either.** Its prescribed fix (`default=False` at `eu_sovereign.py:77`) is doubly wrong: the dict is `str → str`, so Polars silently coerces `False` to the **string `'false'`** (measured: `default=None → ['EUR', None, None]`; `default=False → ['EUR', 'false', 'false']`), meaning it would only ever "work" by accident of truthiness — and the semantics it aims at were refuted by the oracle in the 2026-08-13 attempt, because treating an unknown domestic currency as *foreign* floors every non-EU institution transacting in its own currency. Its one durable contribution is the consumer census: `risk_weights.py`'s negated use is the **only** negated consumer in `src/`, verified independently, with every other consumer taking the positive form where NULL already falls to the conservative branch. Its claimed measurement "`local_currency=None, country=DE, currency=USD` → 1.50 = what a fixed `default=False` looks like" is **not evidence of anything** — DE *is* in the EU map, so `replace_strict` returns `"EUR"`, `.eq("USD")` is a deterministic False, and the `default=None` path is never taken; that row behaves identically before and after. *Original bullet text follows for provenance:* (**MED — ANTI-CONSERVATIVE, silent**) **`engine/eu_sovereign.py:74-79` uses `replace_strict(_EU_COUNTRY_DOMESTIC_CURRENCY, default=None)`, so `is_eu_domestic` is NULL — not False — for every non-EU country.** Then `is_domestic_currency = is_uk_domestic | is_eu_domestic` = `False | NULL` = **NULL**, `~NULL` = NULL, and `_floor_applies` at `risk_weights.py:1514` nulls out so `pl.when(NULL)` takes the `otherwise` branch. Net: **the PS1/26 Art. 121(6) sovereign floor silently never fires for any non-UK/non-EU institution row lacking `cp_local_currency`, in BOTH regimes including the B31 SCRA arm.** Measured in both directions: `local_currency=None, country=US` → 1.00 (the defect suppressing the floor); `local_currency=None, country=DE, currency=USD` → 1.50 (what a fixed `default=False` looks like). **Precisely scoped:** `risk_weights.py:1499` is the **only negated use** in `src/` — every other consumer (`stages/classify/approach.py:304`, `irb/guarantee.py:329`, `crm/guarantees.py:584`, `sa/rw_adjustments.py:787`, `risk_weights.py:1070/1266`) uses the positive form, where NULL falls through to the conservative branch. So the item is a narrow fix at `eu_sovereign.py:77` / `risk_weights.py:1499`, not a broad `is_eu_domestic` rework. Found while auditing P1.316; independently confirmed by a second agent. **Understatement. Effort: S**
- **P1.324** [ ] **RESCOPED 2026-08-15 (LOW → MED) — ⚠ NEEDS OPERATOR SIGN-OFF. The bullet's direction is WRONG: measured, its prescribed fix is RWA-REDUCING, not a conservative over-statement. And its prescribed fix SITE would silently disable a HIGHER floor.**
  - **DIRECTION, MEASURED not reasoned** (`apply_irb_formulas`, EAD 500,000, M 5y; anchored against a full `PipelineOrchestrator` run of a real commercial-only-secured row, byte-identical at 281,994.63 B31 / 298,914.31 CRR). Δ = RWA as `retail_other` minus RWA as `retail_mortgage`, i.e. the effect of the prescribed fix: PD 0.05% **−38.0%** (B31) / +6.4% (CRR); PD 0.10% +4.4%; **crossover at PD ≈ 0.229%**; PD 1% **−18.8%**; PD 3% **−43.9%**; PD 10% **−63.0%**, both regimes. The Art. 154(1) retail-other curve exceeds 0.15 only *below* PD ≈ 0.229%, so across the whole of a realistic secured retail book the bullet's fix **releases capital**. The 10% RWEA floor never acts as the increasing limb — at these PDs modelled RW is 56%+ and `mortgage_rw_floor_adjustment` measured **0.0**. The single RWA-increasing limb is the LGD floor, and only where own-LGD sits below it (40%-covered, own LGD 3%: 0.100 → 0.220, +115%…+130%).
  - ⚠ **THE PRESCRIBED FIX SITE IS WRONG — do not move the class.** Three independently sufficient reasons: **(1)** Art. 154(3) is **not** residential-confined in either regime — PS1/26 p.104 and CRR p.151 both read *"For retail exposures secured by **immovable property collateral** a coefficient of correlation (R) of 0.15…"*, so commercial-secured retail is genuinely entitled to 0.15 and the class is the engine's only route to it (`formulas.py:777-778`, `:1065`). **(2)** CRR Art. 164(4) carries an explicit **commercial** limb that is **HIGHER** than the residential one (`crr.pdf` p.160: *"…secured by residential property … not lower than **10%**. …secured by **commercial immovable property** … not lower than **15%**"*), implemented at `engine/aggregator/_lgd_floor_check.py:87-99` over a population bucketed by `property_type == "commercial"` **within** `retail_mortgage` — narrowing the class **silently empties that bucket and the 15% floor stops firing**. **(3)** Blast radius: `re_split/flagging.py:198-209` *excludes* `RETAIL_MORTGAGE` from the loan splitter, so reclassified rows become newly splitter-eligible (changing the SA leg, which per LESSONS D1 feeds the B31 output floor), and `reporting/pillar3/templates.py:833-840` defines the CR9 leaves `retail_cre_sme` / `retail_cre_non_sme` as `CR9ClassSpec(("retail_mortgage",), property_type="commercial")`, which narrowing makes structurally unreachable.
  - **CORRECTED PREMISE — B31-only, three surgical gates, class untouched.** The defect is not an over-inclusive class; it is that three **B31 residential-limbed** consequences read the class instead of a residential-collateral carrier. `residential_collateral_value` is **already on `IRB_BRANCH_EDGE`** — no schema or edge work. (Prefer it to `property_type`, a single joined scalar at `enrich.py:618` that is unreliable for multi-collateral rows.) **(A)** Art. 154(4A)(b) 10% RWEA floor, `engine/irb/adjustments.py:235-237` — add `residential_collateral_value > 0` to the gate; **RWA-REDUCING**; leave the UK limb alone (ORC-142 / P1.337) and keep the `:232-233` comment recording it as unimplementable. **(B)** Art. 163(1)(b) PD floor, `engine/irb/formulas.py:194-195` — `MORTGAGE|RESIDENTIAL` → 0.001 where commercial-only-secured is owed Art. 163(1)(c) 0.0005; **RWA-REDUCING**; CRR untouched (flat 0.03%). **(C)** Art. 164(4)(a)/(c) LGD floor, `formulas.py:242-250`, `:290-306`, and the blend deny-list at `:520` — not within (a) ⇒ owed the (c) LGD\* blend (LGDU 30% / LGDS 10% immovable); measured at 40% CRE coverage the engine gives **0.100** against a correct **0.220**; **RWA-INCREASING**, the only limb that adds capital.
  - **Verbatim, and NARROWER than the bullet says** — PS1/26 p.104 Art. 154(4A)(b): *"…retail exposures secured by **UK residential** immovable property…"*; Art. 147(5B) p.90: *"…secured by **residential** immovable property"*; Art. 163(1)(b) p.114: *"0.1% for retail exposures secured by residential immovable property **located in the UK**"*.
  - **Chain corrections:** every cited line range has drifted — real sites are `enrich.py:374-482` (docstring `:384-385`), `attributes.py:633-647`, `subtypes.py:211-219` — and the bullet **misses a second mapping site**, `subtypes.py:531-545` (corporate→retail reclassification via `_build_has_property_expr` at `:684-700`).
  - **Defect-pinning:** `tests/unit/test_corporate_to_retail_reclassification.py:524` and `:807` both **absolute**, both would correctly block a class-level change, both **pass unchanged** under the corrected premise. `tests/unit/test_irb_post_model_adjustments.py:670` needs its docstring corrected — it calls this residual "conservative", which the measurement refutes.
  - ⚠ **UNOBSERVABLE TODAY.** All seven registered reporting portfolios × both regimes: commercial-only-secured retail = **0** in every one. Needs a new fixture registered in `RUNS` **and** its builder in `generate_all.py`. Shape: GB natural person + term loan + one `collateral_type="real_estate"`, `property_type="commercial"`, `is_eligible_irb_collateral=True`, and a `model_permissions` row with `exposure_class="retail_mortgage"` (an `="retail"` permission does **not** match — routes to SA with CLS006). Two-leg: a residential sibling that must not move, plus a **partially**-covered low-own-LGD variant so limb (C)'s increase is exercised (a fully-covered row leaves the LGD floor inert, measured). **Effort: M, RWA-REDUCING on two of three limbs, needs sign-off** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 154(4A)(b), 147(5B), 163(1)(b)/(c), 164(4); CRR Art. 154(3), 164(4)
- **P1.362** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-15, found by the P1.324 audit; deliberately NOT folded in)** — **The SA real-estate branch appears insensitive to property kind for a retail-secured row.** Measured: a commercial-only-secured retail row takes SA RW **0.35** (CRR) / **0.4475** (B31) *identically* whether `property_type` is `"commercial"` or `"residential"`. If the SA RE preferential weights are residential-limbed, that is a material understatement against the 75% retail weight. **Stated as an unverified magnitude, deliberately**: the auditor could not build a clean counterfactual, because removing the collateral also removes the CRM relief, so the delta cannot be isolated by that route. Needs a proper design that varies only the property kind. **Effort: M** | Ref: CRR Art. 125/126; PS1/26 Art. 124F/124H · Ev: measured on `PipelineOrchestrator`, both regimes
- **P1.325** [x] **FIXED 2026-08-13** (`P1.336` closed as its duplicate) — Art. 154(4A)'s chapeau makes (a)/(b)/(c) **additive increases to one pre-floor base**, and limb (b) is a TEST evaluated "following application of any post model adjustments calculated under point (b) of Article 146(3)". The engine applied the floor first and scaled it by both scalars. **BOTH BULLETS SHARED A SCOPE ERROR:** they treat `unrecognised_exposure_scalar` as a 146(3)(b) PMA, but Art. 154(4A)(c) is "calculated under **Article 166D(6)**" — a different provision — so it must stay OUTSIDE the floor test. Correct order: (a) on the base, (b) tested against base+(a), (c) on the base added outside. **Direction: RWA-REDUCING** — measured 115,000 → 103,000 (−10.4%) at pma 0.10 / unrec 0.05 on a 6%-RW mortgage; the floor now binds at exactly its 10%-of-exposure-value minimum instead of overshooting. Fully dormant (all scalars default 0.0, `enabled=False`, nothing outside the test helper sets one), so no golden can see it. The engine docstring AND four unit tests — including a class named `TestPMASequencing` whose methods asserted "uses post floor rwa" — stated the inverted order **and attributed it to the article**; all corrected. · *Original bullet:* (LOW — over-statement, dormant) **The Art. 154(4A) PMA/floor ordering is inverted.** The article computes the (b) floor "**following application of any post model adjustments** calculated under point (b) of Article 146(3)"; the engine applies the floor **first** and then multiplies the post-floor RWEA by `pma_rwa_scalar` and `unrecognised_exposure_scalar` (`engine/irb/adjustments.py:228-249`), and the docstring at `:138-144` asserts that ordering as deliberate. On the article's plain reading this over-states. **Dormant on defaults** — both scalars default to `Decimal("0.0")` (`contracts/config.py:331,332`) — but measured live: at `pma_rwa_scalar = 0.10` the P1.319 frame's reduction scales from −2,800,000 to −3,080,000, i.e. the absolute effect scales by `(1 + pma_rwa_scalar + unrecognised_exposure_scalar)` while the proportional effect is invariant. Deliberately excluded from P1.319. **Effort: S** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 154(4A) chapeau + (b)
- **P1.326** [ ] (MED — **UNDERSTATEMENT if P1.316 lands without it**) **CRR Art. 121(4) trade-finance rates are unimplemented and have no pack entry.** Art. 121(4) (verbatim, `crr.pdf` PAGE_INDEX 119): "Notwithstanding paragraphs 2 and 3, for trade finance exposures referred to in point (b) of the second subparagraph of Article 162(3) to unrated institutions, the risk weight shall be **50%** and where the residual maturity … is three months or less, **20%**." `rg` over `src/rwa_calc/rulebook/packs/` finds **no** entry for either rate (only `firb_trade_lc_ccf` and the Art. 162(3) one-day maturity floor). Today such rows take the 100% unrated fallback — wrong but conservative. **This becomes a live 30pp understatement the moment the P1.316 Table 5 ladder lands without a trade-finance exclusion**, because a CQS-1 trade LC would drop to 20% against a required 50%. The carrier exists and is real: `is_short_term_trade_lc` is on two sealed edges (`contracts/edges.py:614`, `:1408`), produced at `stages/hierarchy/facility_undrawn.py:526`, broadcast at `enrich.py:1049-1080`, and already consumed by two SA risk-weight branches and the floor exemption. **Effort: M** | Ref: CRR Art. 121(4); CRR Art. 162(3) second sub-paragraph point (b)
- **P1.327** [x] **FIXED 2026-08-15** (batch 20260815-1) — **RESCOPED: the bullet's "nobody reads it" was false TWICE, and its `arch_metrics.json` suggestion would have built a gate that passes green on the exact failure it exists to catch.** `tests/properties/test_template_row_axis.py` already computes the identical residual over the identical row refs and asserts `abs(residual) <= 0.005` in the dev-loop suite, with three causes registered `xfail(strict=True)`; and `scripts/template_cell_coverage_baseline.json::caveats` already banks all seven warning strings in git. **The real gap was that `_check_baseline` wrote the caveats block and never compared it** (`check_template_cell_coverage.py:481-482` diffed only `live_columns`/`dead_columns`). **Is a non-zero residual a defect or an artefact? SETTLED — a defect, with no legitimate tolerance.** The published COREP C 02.00 IRB axis (`docs/assets/crr-annex-ii-reporting-instructins.pdf` PAGE_INDEX 29-30, pymupdf-extracted) carries a dedicated row for **both** stranding classes: `central governments and central banks` at F-IRB **0260** / A-IRB **0320**, and `Corporates - SME` at F-IRB **0280** / A-IRB **0340**. Every observed residual is RWEA the template *requires* broken out and this repo does not break out, so a magnitude ratchet or per-portfolio tolerance would be the wrong shape. Re-measured at HEAD: **53,723,463.87 across 5 of 28 runs** (the other 23 exactly 0.00), decomposing to the cent into two causes — `corporate_sme` has no key in `_firb_rows`/`_airb_corp_rows`, and `central_govt_central_bank` reads the phantom key `"central_government"` on A-IRB with no F-IRB row at all. - **P1.355** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-15, batch 20260815-1, residual left standing by P1.320)** — **The QRRE obligor aggregate still over-counts a multi-option facility across the root/sub grain.** P1.320 fixed the **leg-multiplication** grain (each leg of one facility contributing the facility's limit once per row). It did **not** fix the second grain: drawn legs key on the **sub**-facility (`facility_limit` joined at `engine/stages/hierarchy/enrich.py:713`) while the root's waterfall legs key on the **root** (`facility_undrawn.py:561`), so a sub-limit is counted alongside the root limit that already contains it. Measured on real `HierarchyResolver` output — one individual, one 50,000 MOF root, two 30,000 committed subs, 10,000 drawn under `SUBA`: **current 130,000 → post-P1.320 80,000, where the economic aggregate nominal is 50,000.** **Conservative** (an over-count denies QRRE, so it is RWA-increasing) which is why it was not folded into P1.320 — a fix here is RWA-REDUCING and needs its own evidence and sign-off. Note the trap recorded in the P1.320 design §2.1: `root_facility_reference` keying is **not** the obvious answer — legs under one root carry different limits, so root-keying needs an intra-root aggregation, and `max` silently discards every sub-limit, which under-counts (RWA-reducing) whenever the root limit is null or understated, and no validator enforces `sum(sub limits) ≤ root limit`. Any fix must state which of those it assumes. Pinned today by the P1.320 leg (v) fixture, whose docstring records that it pins the leg-multiplication grain **only**. **Effort: M** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 147(5A)(c); CRR Art. 154(4)(c) · Ev: `engine/stages/classify/subtypes.py:186-197`; `engine/stages/hierarchy/enrich.py:713`; `engine/stages/hierarchy/facility_undrawn.py:561`

- **P1.352** [x] **CLOSED 2026-08-15 as SUPERSEDED — the prescribed fix cannot achieve its stated purpose, and executing it would have produced a fixture that proves nothing.** The reachability measurement is CONFIRMED and now larger: re-counted at HEAD across `RUNS` (8 portfolios × 2 regimes) + `CORPUS` (6 × 2) = **28 runs**, `other_*` rows = 2, `other_residual_lease` = **0**; the string does not occur anywhere in `tests/fixtures/`. "Its entire gate was one unit file" is also CONFIRMED — only `tests/unit/test_other_items_risk_weights.py` reddens on a P1.289 revert, and `classification_table.toml`'s `SA14-other-items` asserts the exposure **class** only, so it passes under any risk weight. **But the branch is DEGENERATE IN PRODUCTION and a fixture row cannot exercise it.** `residual_maturity_years` is declared only on `collateral` / `ccr_collateral` / `sft_collateral`; on the exposure frame `SA_INPUT_CONTRACT` (`engine/sa/risk_weights.py:165`) injects it as a **typed null** and nothing populates it. So `_leased_residual_rw_expr` computes `1/max(1, ceil(1.0 − 0.5))` = **100% for every production row, both regimes** — measured end-to-end with 1.6 / 2.6 / 5.0-year rows, all returning `residual_maturity_years = None`, `risk_weight = 1.0`. A fixture row added today lands at exactly 100%, indistinguishable from generic Art. 134(2) OTHER **and invariant under a P1.289 revert**. ⚠ The bullet's own "largest observable delta" framing walks into the same trap: `y = 1.49` has the biggest delta (+32.89pp) but `ceil(1.49 − 0.5) = 1`, so its post-fix RW is **100%** — the degenerate value. Superseded by **P1.360** (the carrier, capital) and **P1.361** (the fixture, blocked on it). Also confirmed: the bullet's `ead_final` warning is right — Art. 134(7)'s first sentence sets the exposure value for the *receivable* leg (discounted MLP) and its last sentence gives the *residual* leg its own `1/t × 100% × residual value`; they are different legs and getting it backwards would exercise the wrong limb.
- **P1.360** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-15, the capital half of P1.352) — Art. 134(7)'s `t` has NO production input, so the `1/t` residual-lease branch is degenerate at 100%.** Verbatim, CRR Art. 134(7) (`crr.pdf` PAGE_INDEX 131) and PS1/26 Art. 134(7) (`ps126app1.pdf` PAGE_INDEX 68, word-for-word identical, `[Note: This rule corresponds to Article 134 of CRR…]` — the provision survives): *"When the exposure is a residual value of leased assets, the risk-weighted exposure amounts shall be calculated as follows: **1/t \* 100 % \* residual value**, where t is the greater of 1 and the nearest number of whole years of the lease remaining."* No exposure-side raw table can carry that term. **Fix:** add a **lease-scoped** `lease_remaining_term_years` (`ColumnSpec(pl.Float64, required=False)`) to `LOAN_SCHEMA` (beside the lease-convention comment at `data/schemas.py:637-653`) and `RAW_TABLE_EDGES["loans"]`, add it to `SA_INPUT_CONTRACT` so it injects as a typed null when absent, and have `_leased_residual_rw_expr` read it — the null path keeps today's conservative 100%, so `test_residual_lease_null_maturity_defaults_to_hundred` stays green and stays correct. ⚠⚠ **DO NOT reuse or globally populate `residual_maturity_years`, and do not derive it from `(maturity_date − reporting_date)` mirroring `risk_weights.py:997-1010`.** It has TWO other consumers, with OPPOSITE signs, both currently inert because the column is constant-null: (i) `risk_weights.py:853`, the CRR Art. 120(2) Table 4 rated-institution short-term gate — populating it newly grants preferential weights to every rated institution with < 3 months *remaining* but a longer *original* term, **RWA-REDUCING across the whole SA institution book**, and per LESSONS D1 the SA pipeline runs unconditionally to feed the Basel 3.1 output floor, so it lowers the floor for IRB books too; (ii) `engine/crm/simple_method.py:317,507,730-745`, the Art. 239(1) FCSM maturity-mismatch gate which zeroes collateral when `coll_residual < exp_residual` — populating it newly **zeroes collateral, RWA-INCREASING**, on a path whose `fcsm` portfolio is deliberately *not* in `RUNS` and so would land completely unobserved. **Direction: RWA-REDUCING as a capability** (a correct 5-year lease is 20% against today's 100%, an 80pp reduction on the residual value) but **number-neutral on today's estate**, since no feed can supply the field. ⚠ The loader seal strips the column silently — verified, a loans frame carrying `residual_maturity_years=2.6` reached the pipeline without it and `result.errors` was empty, so "nobody has reported this" is not evidence. Resolve the in-tree docs conflict in the same change: `docs/data-model/input-schemas.md:114` says `other_residual_lease` → "100% RW" (today's degenerate behaviour) while `:366-367` says "1/t × 100%" (the rule). **Effort: M** | Ref: CRR Art. 134(7); PS1/26 Art. 134(7) · Ev: `engine/sa/risk_weights.py:165,464-497`; `data/schemas.py:637-653`; `contracts/edges.py:1769-1782`
- **P1.361** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-15, the coverage half of P1.352) — BLOCKED ON P1.360.** Art. 134(7) has zero production reachability (0 `other_residual_lease` rows across 28 registered runs) and its entire gate is one unit file. Build the fixture **after** the carrier lands, or it asserts the degenerate 100%. **Two-leg, with the values derived**: `t = max(1, ceil(y − 0.5))`, `RW = 1/t`, against the pre-P1.289 `1/max(1, y)`. **SURVIVOR** `y = 5.0`, residual value 4,000,000 → `ceil(4.5) = 5`, RW 20%, RWA **800,000** under both formulas — proves the column is populated, since a null would give 100%, and keeps the cell live afterwards. **MOVER** `y = 1.6`, residual value 2,000,000 → RW **50%** / RWA 1,000,000 post-fix, 62.5% / 1,250,000 pre-fix, **100% / 2,000,000 if the carrier is unwired** — three distinguishable states, which is why 1.6 is chosen over the larger-delta 1.49 (whose post-fix RW is the degenerate 100%). Optional third leg `y = 2.5` pins the tie direction (50% post, 40% pre, 33.33% under round-ties-up). **ZERO-GOLDEN-MOVEMENT HOME:** a NEW `tests/fixtures/reporting_other_items_portfolio.py` registered in `RUNS`, following the `re-split` / `crm-substitution` / `art199` precedent of a registered portfolio with no `tests/expected_outputs/reporting/<name>/` directory. Perturbing `rich` (46 + 51 golden frames) or `sa-classes` (18 + 21) would be operator-gated regeneration for **zero** coverage gain, since the cell census ratchets `(template, column)` and a new *row* lights no new column — `rich` already emits the other-items sheet. Use `_sa_config` with no IRB permission (an IRB permission routes the residual leg away from `apply_risk_weights`) and **state that in the module docstring** — this is exactly the "registered, wrong config, dead cell" third form of LESSONS B5. Registration: `RUNS` (mandatory); `template_cell_coverage_baseline.json` **only if** a column moves dead→live; `validation_known_breaks.json` if breaks/vacuity change; **NOT** `generate_all.py` — reporting portfolios are code-built DataFrames, not parquet. *Correction to a widely-repeated belief, verified in this audit:* a `RUNS` addition does **not** hard-exit the cell census on run count — `EXPECTED_RUNS` is computed from `len(RUNS)` at import (`check_template_cell_coverage.py:304`) so it self-satisfies, and `--check` diffs only the `live_columns`/`dead_columns` sets; the `counts`/`provenance` blocks are informational. **Effort: S after P1.360** | Ref: CRR Art. 134(7); PS1/26 Art. 134(7)

- **P1.350** [ ] **RESCOPED 2026-08-15 — ⚠ NEEDS OPERATOR SIGN-OFF (RWA-REDUCING). The rule reading is RIGHT; the bullet's reason for parking it is REFUTED by measurement.** Verbatim, PS1/26 Art. 123 (`ps126app1.pdf` p.idx 46-47): *"**3.** Subject to paragraph 4, retail exposures shall be assigned the following risk weights: (a) regulatory retail exposures that are transactor exposures shall be assigned a risk weight of 45%; …"* and *"**4.** Retail exposures arising due to loans granted … against the unconditional transfer of part of the borrower's pension or salary … shall be assigned a risk weight of 35%, provided that all the following conditions are met: (a)…(d)"*. So paragraph 4 overrides the **whole** of the paragraph-3 ladder including the 45%. The engine appends the transactor limb first (`engine/sa/risk_weights.py:603-614`). **The bullet's "population looks negligible / more likely a data error / the right answer may be a validation error" is WRONG on all three counts:** `is_qrre_transactor` is **not gated on `is_revolving` anywhere upstream** — it is a free-standing optional Boolean on `FACILITY_SCHEMA:501` with no cross-field constraint and no validator mentioning it; the classifier gates the QRRE *class* on `is_revolving` but the risk-weight limb keys on the **flag**, deliberately (P1.293). **Two reachable paths, and the second needs no odd input at all:** (A) a facility row carrying both flags, whose `_UNDRAWN` child carries both directly; **(B) `engine/stages/hierarchy/enrich.py:695` coalesces the facility's `is_qrre_transactor` onto EVERY child exposure unconditionally**, so a payroll term loan mapped under a credit-card facility inherits it without anyone ever setting both flags on one input row. **Measured, full `PipelineOrchestrator`, natural-person obligor, payroll flag on the LOAN only: CRR → 0.35, Basel 3.1 → 0.45, schema-valid, zero errors.** Regime-inconsistent on identical input, and B31 is the wrong one. So the re-ordering is **not dead code** and **a validation error would be wrong** — Art. 123(4) grants the 35% to any retail exposure meeting (a)-(d), so a firm attesting both is making a claim the rule recognises. **Scope is NARROWER than the bullet:** payroll already outranks the (b) 75% and (c) 100% limbs, so exactly **one cell per retail class** moves — `(transactor=T, payroll=T, qualifies=T)` 0.45 → 0.35; the other 21 cells are unchanged and CRR is unchanged in all 24. **SPEC, ready to execute on sign-off:** move the payroll `.when()/.then()` pair to be the **first** limb of `_b31_append_retail_branches`; **do NOT** conjoin it with `qualifies` (Art. 123(4) says "Retail exposures", not "regulatory retail" — adding it would break the already-correct `(T,T,F) = 0.35` cell and raise it to 100%); **do NOT** touch `_crr_append_retail_branches` (CRR has no "subject to" chapeau and no transactor limb at all); **do NOT** add a validation error. Reachability in the estate is **ZERO** (no fixture sets `is_payroll_loan` on a facility), so **no golden should move — if one does, stop, something other than this cell changed.** Defect-pinning: `tests/unit/test_b31_sa_risk_weights.py:3835` `test_payroll_loan_qrre_transactor_gets_45pct` is **the LESSONS C1 shape exactly** — it states the code's own ordering as its justification and cites **no article** for the precedence, i.e. test and code written from the same sentence; its assertion is absolute so it reddens rather than passing through, and must be inverted deliberately. (The bullet's `Ev:` pointer to `:3723-3724` drifted; the real test is at 3835-3859.) Ratchet note: `risk_weights.py` is at 1689 against a 1709 cap — 20 lines, comments count. **Effort: S, but RWA-REDUCING and it lowers the SA-equivalent RW feeding the B31 output floor** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 123(3) chapeau, Art. 123(4)

Residuals filed: P1.346 (uncapped Art. 222(3) blend), P1.347 (the FCSM portfolio is withheld from `RUNS`). · *Original bullet:* **Art. 197 eligibility gate is not applied on the Art. 222 Financial Collateral Simple Method path — UNDERSTATES capital, worst case 33.3%** — `crm/processor.py` runs `compute_fcsm_columns` at Step 3.8 BEFORE `apply_haircuts` at Step 4, and `apply_haircuts` (`crm/haircuts.py:337-371`, `_bond_ineligible` / `_equity_listing_ineligible` / `_cln_ineligible`) is the ONLY place the engine overrides the firm-supplied `is_eligible_financial_collateral`. `crm/simple_method.py:455` filters on the raw attestation alone, so the Simple Method recognises collateral the Comprehensive Method rejects, at the collateral's own SA risk weight floored at 20%. ORC-282 is the passing Comprehensive-Method control that localises the defect to one method. **A fix is NOT a step reorder** — Step 3.8 must precede the Comprehensive computation, which IRB LGD still needs; factor the Art. 197 gate out of `apply_haircuts` and apply it to the FCSM input too.
