- **P1.345** [x] **FIXED 2026-08-15** (batch 20260815-1) — **RESCOPED at audit: the bullet's central claim was false, and the gate that actually let P1.317 through was in PRODUCTION, not in tests.** Measured by injecting `rwa_final = None` into every row the shared `find_loan_rows` helper returns and re-running all 16 files that use the idiom: **51 of 65 tests FAIL**, including both files the bullet names. On the one-row populations these scenarios build, a null collapses the guarded sum to `0` and every absolute equality assertion (`== approx(650_000)`) reddens — so "a recurrence would leave them green" was wrong for the cited instances. **The discriminator is not the guard but the ASSERTION SHAPE**: the guard is inert against equality-to-constant and against an inequality between two equally-mutated quantities, and defect-tolerating only against a **one-sided inequality versus a constant**. Pattern sweep (AST scan of `tests/` + greps for `or 0.0` / `fill_null` / `drop_nulls` / `filter(None, …)`): **18 sites in 16 files**, of which **2** are genuinely defect-tolerating, 16 are harmless (measured red under the mutation), and a further 11 template-cell reads are deliberate (`None` legitimately means "cell not published"). **Three legs shipped:**
  - **Leg C, the item** — `_AGG_BOUND_SPECS` (`contracts/validation.py`) carried a null spec for `ead_final` and **no `rwa_final` twin**. P1.317 published a populated `ead_final`, `risk_weight = 2.5` and a null `rwa_final`, and OUT001–003 all guard on `is_not_null()` first, so every bound passed it. Added **OUT005** (`ERROR_RWA_NULL`) as one tuple entry plus a predicate branch. It runs at the pipeline exit on **every** run and is **class-agnostic**, where P1.317's own shipped guard (`test_cell_rederivation.py::test_every_equity_leg_carries_its_rwea_to_rwa_final`) is equity-scoped and passes a null on any other class. Fail-first evidence recorded: on the exact P1.317 shape OUT005 fires **alone** and OUT004 stays correctly silent because `ead_final` is populated. Pinned by `tests/contracts/test_aggregated_bundle_validation.py::TestOut005NullRwaFinal` (3 tests, incl. an independence test proving neither code masks the other). **Blast radius measured zero** — 0 `rwa_final` nulls across 163 rows in all 16 registered `RUNS` portfolios, both regimes.
  - **Leg B** — the non-null assertion went at the **single choke point** (`tests/acceptance/p1_190_pipeline_helpers.py::find_loan_rows`, imported by **29** files) rather than in 18 places, guarded on column presence since `slotting_results` need not carry `rwa_final`. ⚠ **The bullet's first prescription ("drop the guard so a null reddens") was rejected and the reason is recorded at the site**: an unguarded sum raises `TypeError: unsupported operand type(s) for +: 'int' and 'NoneType'`, a strictly worse diagnostic than a named assertion.
  - **Leg A** — the 2 genuinely defect-tolerating sites, `tests/acceptance/{basel31,crr}/test_p1_270_art_194_own_issue_collateral.py:58`, `assert _rwa(...) < GROSS_RWA` → `assert 0 < _rwa(...) < GROSS_RWA`. Note the existing bound is 12.5× looser than the actual value (measured 800,000 CRR / 600,000 B31 against `GROSS_RWA = 10,000,000`), so it also tolerates grossly over-applied CRM relief independently of nulls — **pinning the absolute value needs a hand-calc and was deliberately NOT done from a measurement**; filed as P5.55.
  ⚠ **A syntactic `arch_check` rule banning `is not None` before a sum was considered and REJECTED**: it would force 16 pointless edits on the harmless majority and would miss the dominant form entirely, since `pl.col(x).sum()` and `Series.sum()` skip nulls by design throughout `engine/aggregator/`. A lint on the Python-level idiom polices the rarest shape of the hazard. Number-neutral; full estate green (8544 + 3047 passed, arch_check exit 0). · *Original bullet:* **NEW (2026-08-13, found by the P1.317 adversarial review)** — a defect-tolerating helper of the exact shape that hid P1.317 survives in two acceptance tests.

- **P1.333** [~] **ATTEMPTED AND REVERTED 2026-08-13 — the prescribed fix is REFUTED BY THE ORACLE. Do not re-attempt `fill_null(False)`.** The one-line fix all three filings prescribe (`is_domestic_currency = (is_uk_domestic | is_eu_domestic).fill_null(False)`) was implemented, passed its own purpose-built test and the whole unit tree, **and broke three oracle cases** — ORC-052, ORC-053, ORC-055 (`tests/oracle/`, the human-reviewed golden referee). Reverting that single file returned the oracle to 211 passed, so attribution is exact.
  - **WHY IT IS WRONG.** `fill_null(False)` collapses *"the obligor's domestic currency is unknown"* into *"the exposure is foreign"*. The domestic-currency map knows only `GB → GBP` and the EU members → `EUR`; it does **not** know that USD is the domestic currency of the US. So every non-EU institution transacting **in its own currency** was newly floored. The oracle's institution cases use `{"country_code": "US", "currency": "USD"}` — a US bank in USD, which is *domestic* — and correctly expect the Art. 121(2) Table 5 SCRA weights (Grade A 40%, B 75%). The fix raised them to 100%. Note the oracle helper is named `_FOREIGN`, meaning *foreign to the UK reporting entity*, not *foreign currency* — that naming is what makes the shape easy to misread.
  - **MEASURED, three shapes, Basel 3.1, SCRA Grade A, unrated sovereign:**

    | shape | `risk_weight` | branch reason |
    |---|---|---|
    | US/USD, `cp_local_currency` **absent** (the oracle shape) | **1.00** ← the defect the fix introduces | `floor_bound` |
    | US/USD, `cp_local_currency="USD"` (explicitly domestic) | **0.40** ← already correct today | `domestic_currency` |
    | US/EUR, `cp_local_currency="USD"` (genuinely foreign) | 1.00 ← already correct today | `floor_bound` |

    Row 2 is decisive: **the engine already gets this right whenever the data is present.** The gap is not a missing `fill_null`, it is a missing input.
  - **THE PREMISE "SILENTLY DEAD" IS ALSO WRONG.** The Kleene null is a defensible encoding of *"the FX test is undecidable on this row"*, and it is **not silent** — since the Phase 3 branch-reason work those rows are named `UNKNOWN_FALLBACK` and carry a `BR001` warning, and `scripts/branch_census_baseline.json` pins the limb. The three-state distinction (known-domestic / known-foreign / **undecidable**) is real regulatory content, and any fix that collapses it to two states will be wrong on one of them.
  - **CORRECT DIRECTIONS, neither of which is Effort S.** (a) **Supply the missing data** — a cited jurisdiction → domestic-currency table in the rulepack, extending the UK/EU map to the jurisdictions the book actually holds. This is a regulatory-data question (what *is* the domestic currency of each jurisdiction, and does the PRA define it) and belongs in a pack with a `Citation`, not in engine code. It makes rows 1 and 2 of the table above agree. (b) **Require the input** — treat an unknown domestic currency on an unrated institution exposure as a **data-quality error** (the error channel, per the project's error-accumulation rule) rather than silently choosing a risk weight in either direction. (a) and (b) compose: supply what is knowable, and refuse to guess the rest.
  - **DO NOT** re-file this as "add `fill_null(False)`", and do not use P1.323b's `default=False` at `eu_sovereign.py:77` either — the dict is `str → str` and Polars silently coerces `False` to the **string `'false'`**, so it would "work" by accident of truthiness and still mis-classify the same population.
  - **P1.312 IS REFUTED and closes here** (see its own bullet): the absent-column asymmetry it describes does not exist — `ensure_columns` fills `cp_sovereign_cqs` and `cp_local_currency` from `SA_INPUT_CONTRACT`, measured on the exact frame it cites. **P1.323b remains open and is the same defect as this bullet**; close them together.
  - **P1.334 stays open and is now MORE urgent, not less.** It records that UK CRR Art. 121 has four paragraphs and contains no (5) or (6), so the floor should not apply under CRR at all. Any future attempt here collides with `tests/acceptance/crr/test_p1_121_art_121_3_unrated_inst_short_term.py` (VN counterparty, 85-day loan), which moves `0.2 → 1.0` — so **P1.334's regime gate must land in the same change**, or that pin has to be re-baselined to a value P1.334 says is wrong.
  - The attempt's diff is preserved outside the repo for reference; the fail-first test it came with (`test_p1_333_art_121_6_kleene_null_domesticity.py`) was removed with it, but its **discrimination design is worth rebuilding**: a 21-cell SCRA × sovereign-CQS grid where 9 cells move and 12 must not, so a fix that floors everything at the Art. 114(1) residual breaks 5 of them and a fix that *assigns* rather than *floors* breaks the whole Grade C row.
  - *Original bullet text follows for provenance:* **Art. 121(6) sovereign floor for institutions is silently DEAD for every non-EU counterparty (Kleene null) — ANTI-CONSERVATIVE under Basel 3.1** — `sa/risk_weights.py:959` builds `is_domestic_currency = is_uk_domestic | is_eu_domestic`; `engine/eu_sovereign.py:74-79` returns `country.replace_strict(_EU_COUNTRY_DOMESTIC_CURRENCY, default=None).eq(currency)`, so for a NON-EU country the mapped value is null, `null.eq("USD")` is null, and `False | null = null`. `_apply_sovereign_floor_for_institutions` (`:1496-1514`) then computes `_is_fx = ~is_domestic_currency` = null, `_floor_applies` is null, and `pl.when(null)` takes the `otherwise` branch. Instrumented: `cp_sovereign_cqs=6, currency=USD, cp_country_code=US` gives `is_domestic_currency -> None` and `risk_weight` stays 1.0 while `_sovereign_rw=1.5` — `max_horizontal` never applied. Dead for GB counterparties in a non-GBP currency and for every third-country institution. Under Basel 3.1 that is a live shortfall wherever SCRA Grade A (40%) or B (75%) sits below the jurisdiction's sovereign RW — exactly the population the article exists for. **Why the estate cannot see it**: all 20 sovereign-floor tests supply `local_currency` EXPLICITLY (`test_sovereign_floor_institutions.py`, `test_p1_254_*.py`), taking the deterministic `.is_not_null()` branch at `:1497` and stepping over the null path; `test_missing_columns_backward_compat` (`:382`) records a DIFFERENT asymmetry and was plausibly mistaken for this one. Fix `fill_null(False)` before the `|`, and add a third-country FX leg. **Effort: S** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 121(6) / BCBS CRE20.22

P1.316 implements neither — it excludes trade finance from the ladder via `sovereign_derived.crr_art_121_4_trade_finance_expr()`, so excluded rows land on the Art. 121(2) 100% residual, conservative relative to both readings and pinned by `test_p1_316_trade_finance_stays_on_the_100pct_residual`. Before P1.316 the exposed population (unrated-institution trade finance, maturity > 3 months, sovereign CQS 1) took 20% — a 30pp understatement, and the reason P1.316's earlier attempt was dropped at its Wave-1 gate. Settle from primary text against the PRA Rulebook rendering, then either add an explicit 50% branch or document the literal reading. **Keep the pinning test either way** — before this batch nothing in the estate distinguished 20/50/100 on that population. **Effort: S (research), S (implementation)** | Ref: UK CRR Art. 121(4), Art. 162(3)
- **P1.339** [ ] **b31 OF 07.00 r0341 ("non-SME") absorbs the SME obligor's CRE-secured leg while r0343 ("SME") reads NULL** — observed while lighting the C 07.00 risk-weight rungs; not investigated. If confirmed, the SME/non-SME split of the regulatory-real-estate of-which block is inverted or the SME predicate is not reaching the row. **Effort: S to confirm** | Ref: PS1/26 Annex II, OF 07.00 rows 0340-0354

- **P1.341** [ ] **The branch census's dead limbs — five regulatory limbs no row in the 28-run estate ever takes.** Measured by `scripts/check_branch_census.py` (Phase 3), banked in `scripts/branch_census_baseline.json`, and this bullet is the OWNER every dead entry names. Two are the Art. 121(6) floor's own outcomes (`floor_bound`, `floor_not_binding`) and are owned by **P1.333** instead — the rule's applicable population is empty, so neither outcome is reachable until the Kleene null is fixed. The three this bullet owns are pure fixture gaps, each unit-tested directly but never exercised end to end: `sa_risk_weight_branch_reason::trade_exempt` (Art. 121(6)(b) self-liquidating trade carve-out — `is_short_term_trade_lc` is null or false in every portfolio), `irb_lgd_branch_reason::supervisory_subtype` (Art. 161(1)(e)/(f)/(g) — `purchased_receivables_subtype` is null in every fixture), and `irb_lgd_branch_reason::ccr_modelled` (the P1.215 A-IRB CCR/SFT LGD carrier — the CCR fixture's netting sets are SA-treated, so no synthetic CCR row reaches the IRB LGD selector at all). Each needs one portfolio leg; registering it flips the entry from `dead` to `reached` and the ratchet then guards it. Also owns `irb_lgd_branch_reason::UNKNOWN_FALLBACK`, which is banked dead as the DESIRED state — no IRB row is priced on a supervisory default standing in for absent data — so that limb going live fails the gate. **Effort: S** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 121(6)(b), Art. 161(1)(e)-(g) · Ev: `scripts/branch_census_baseline.json`
- **P1.342** [x] **FIXED 2026-08-13** (`b25bb046`) — the 2% was correct only by accident: `original_currency` is null on CCR/SFT rows minted after the FX converter, so the domesticity test was Kleene-null and the floor never ran. Fixed at the two PRODUCERS, not in the shared `denomination_currency_expr` the bullet named — that has five other consumers and loosening it is RWA-REDUCING. The Art. 306 carve-out carries a TRADE-EXPOSURE term as well as a QCCP one, because CRR Art. 107(2) leaves an ordinary loan to a QCCP inside Art. 121(6); copying the existing QCCP pin predicate would have exempted it. Number-neutral, but the rows now land on a named `qccp_trade_exposure` limb instead of `UNKNOWN_FALLBACK`. Census baseline hand-edited (removals are refused by `--update-baseline` by design), naming P1.333 as the owner that must keep the limb dead. · *Original bullet:* (**HIGH — P1.333's PRESCRIBED FIX IS UNSAFE AS WRITTEN; sequence before it**) **The Art. 121(6) scope predicate does not exclude QCCP trade exposures, and the Kleene null is currently the only thing preventing a 50x overstatement.** Found by the Phase 3 branch census: the two rows in the whole estate that reach the sovereign floor are `ccr__NS_CCR_QCCP` under both regimes, and both land on `UNKNOWN_FALLBACK`. Instrumented and **measured**: applying P1.333's prescribed `fill_null(False)` takes that row from `risk_weight` 0.02 / `rwa_final` 109,933.82 to 1.00 / 5,496,700 — because `_is_institution` (`_upper_class.str.contains("INSTITUTION")`) is True for a QCCP, `cqs` is null so `_is_unrated` is True, and `cp_sovereign_cqs` is null so the floor reads the Art. 114(1) 100% residual. Art. 306 pins a QCCP trade exposure at 2% and is lex specialis; the floor must not reach it. **Second, independent finding on the same row:** the nullity is NOT the non-EU `replace_strict` path P1.333 blames. `cp_country_code` is `GB` and `currency` is `GBP`, so the UK limb should fire — but `denomination_currency_expr` returns `pl.col("original_currency")` whenever that column EXISTS, and CCR rows carry it **null**, so `is_uk_domestic` is `True & null = null`. So there are (at least) two distinct producers of the null: the country-side map default and a currency-side carrier that is null on rows which never went through FX conversion. P1.333's one-line fix addresses neither and merely arms the floor. **Closure requires:** a QCCP/Art. 306 carve-out in the floor's scope, a null-safe denomination-currency selector, and a fixture pinning the 2% survives. **Effort: M** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 121(6), CRR Art. 306, CRE54.14-15 · Ev: `engine/sa/sovereign_floor.py`, `engine/eu_sovereign.py::denomination_currency_expr`

### Tier 2 — Test Coverage Gaps (no code changes, but essential for confidence)

<!-- P5.40-P5.49 below are the GATE gaps found by the correctness-proposal batch
     (2026-08-09). Several were found by adversarial review of gates built in

- **P5.40** [ ] **`PASS -> NOT_EVALUATED` escapes all six supervisory ratchet legs — the sibling of the vacuity hole just closed** — the 0.3 vacuity ratchet closes `PASS -> VACUOUS`; a rule that stops resolving ENTIRELY leaves the PASS population without entering the vacuity population, and the coverage ratchet only fires when a whole TEMPLATE goes unchecked. Measured: dropping ONE emitted row (C 02.00 r0160) from `crr/rich` moves two ERROR rules `PASS -> NOT_EVALUATED`, including `v3335_i` which passed on **rich only** so afterwards it has no verdict anywhere in the 16-run matrix — all six legs green, `templates_uncovered: ()` unmoved. Nulling C 02.00's only value column darkens 8 rules, also green. **Exposure: 221 rules (130 ERROR) hold a real verdict on exactly ONE run**, each one row-drop from going dark. Partial mitigation only: `coverage_report.py --check` ratchets `never_evaluated_rules` (max 785) and `union_binding_rules_*` (min 257/289) and by those definitions the row-drop moves both — but that is inference from the metric definitions, they are COUNTS so one rule going dark nets against another activating, and it is a different gate from the mandatory Tier 2 register. Fix: ratchet the per-rule `(regime, rule_id) -> has-a-verdict-anywhere` SET, not a count. **Effort: M** | Ref: `tests/acceptance/reporting/test_supervisory_validations.py` legs (f)/(g)
- **P5.42** [ ] **Input-domain census: a third form of dead branch that no cell or rule census can see** — distinct from the three forms in the LESSONS Graduation ledger (portfolio unregistered / registered-but-cell-dead / registered-but-wrong-config). Here the REGULATORY INPUT VALUE that activates a branch is absent from every fixture, so the branch is dead at the input-domain level while the cells are populated and the rules pass. Worked example: Art. 121(1) Table 5 was exercised NOWHERE outside `tests/oracle/` — all 16 registered runs carry unrated institutions with a null `cp_sovereign_cqs`, and a scan of all 494 `tests/fixtures/**/*.parquet` found exactly ONE candidate whose loan is ~85 days so Art. 121(3) claims it first. Three wrong risk weights shipped through a ~9,600-test estate structurally unable to see them. Proposed gate: enumerate each pack `LookupTable` / `BandedTable` key domain and assert every key value is realised by at least one fixture row REACHING the branch that reads it; ratchet the unrealised set. This is the most generalisable finding of the batch — it explains how P1.316 survived. **Effort: M** | Ref: `rwa_calc/rulebook/packs/`, `scripts/coverage_report.py` as the model for the ratchet
- **P5.43** [ ] **Add an integrated-pipeline oracle driver — proposal item 1.3, and the batch produced the case for it** — every oracle calls `calculate_branch` directly and bypasses hierarchy, classifier and CRM **by design** (`tests/oracle/test_oracle.py` docstring), so a green oracle cannot show that a fix reaches production. Demonstrated live: the P1.316 Table 5 fix made the oracle suite green and discharged three strict xfails **while the pipeline test failed 11 ways**, every leg taking the `CQS.UNRATED` fallback of 1.0 instead of the ladder. Without the accompanying fixture it would have shipped as a "verified" capital fix that did nothing on a real portfolio, with three discharged xfails standing as the evidence. Note `drivers.run_crm_sa` (added by O3) already runs `CRMProcessor` then the SA branch, so this is an extension rather than a start. Compare a handful of existing hand-derived values through `PipelineOrchestrator` end-to-end. **Effort: M** | Ref: `tests/oracle/drivers.py`, `tests/unit/crr/test_p1_316_art_121_1_table_5_unrated_institution.py::TestP1316ThroughTheFullPipeline`
- **P5.44** [ ] **`output_digest` is not reproducible, so the reachability probe cannot return UNREACHABLE at all** — blocks the whole defect-injection campaign (proposal item 0.1). Three clean computations of the wide digest gave three different values; float summation-order dust in 5 of 5,453 columns (`c08_01/corporate/0310` = 32528268.383276727 vs ...723; `c09_01/TOTAL/0090` = 19580833.333333336 vs ...332; `c09_02/GB/0125` = 43902551.59373399 vs ...996). `scripts/defect_injection.py:366-371` hashes `str(frame.get_column(column).to_list())` — exact equality on `repr(float)` — and the bias is ONE-DIRECTIONAL toward REACHABLE, which collapses the three-verdict design: truly-dead mutants enter the scored denominator, cannot be caught by anything, and surface as PHANTOM ESCAPES. Decisive independent proof: `generic-correlation-decay` reports REACHABLE although `1.0-exp(-50)` and `1.0-exp(-45)` are both exactly 1.0 in float64. **Verified fix**: round floats (6dp) before hashing — the digest becomes reproducible and BOTH controls then behave as the catalogue requires. **Do NOT flip `expect_unreachable` on `control-unreachable-output-floor-full`**: once the digest is fixed the mutant is correctly UNREACHABLE, and flipping it would score a mutant that `tests/unit/rulebook/test_output_floor_features.py:59` catches trivially, inflating the rate. Also fix `generic-retail-correlation-decay`'s `expect_unreachable=True`, which is wrong on its own terms (`1.0-exp(-35)` vs `1.0-exp(-30)` differ by ~1e-13). **Effort: S** | Ref: `scripts/defect_injection.py::output_digest`, `scripts/defect_catalogue.py:361-379`
- **P5.45** [ ] **The injection harness's restore is not byte-exact and its "byte verification" cannot see the one corruption it causes** — `injected()` does `original = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")`, whose universal-newline translation turns CRLF into LF in memory, then writes `original` back on restore — so the restore is **structurally incapable** of putting CRLF back for a CRLF file. And the post-restore check compares `read_text()` against that same already-normalised copy, i.e. a TEXT comparison blind to the corruption; it passed while `engine/irb/formulas.py` differed from HEAD by 1,323 bytes. The docstring's "post-restore verification that the bytes really came back" overstates the code. Blast radius: 2 of 10 mutation targets are CRLF in HEAD (`engine/irb/formulas.py`, `engine/sa/crr_risk_weight_tables.py`), reachable by 8 of 22 mutants. Fix: read and write bytes, and compare bytes. **Effort: S** | Ref: `scripts/defect_injection.py::injected`
- **P5.46** [ ] **Two further `defect_injection.py` defects that corrupt a published scorecard** — (a) a gate **TIMEOUT becomes an ESCAPED**: `_score_mutant`'s `for/else` falls through to ESCAPED when nothing went red, and a timed-out gate is not red, so a mutant whose only catching gate timed out lands in the published escaped list indistinguishable from a real escape; (b) **`--resume` TRUNCATES the scorecard** — it filters the mutants it runs then overwrites `--out` with only the resumed subset, which matters because sharding is the documented fallback if a ladder leg exceeds the 6h runner ceiling, and sharding needs a real combine step. Also: `main()` never creates `--out`'s parent directory and writes only at the very end, so a completed multi-hour campaign can discard every result. `scripts/injection_ratchet.py --check` hard-fails on both symptoms so neither can be banked over. **Effort: S** | Ref: `scripts/defect_injection.py`
- **P5.47** [ ] **No test anywhere imports `defect_injection`, so its safety properties are ungated** — nothing under `tests/` imports it at all, which makes the interpreter override, the spawn pre-flight and the restore machinery correct-by-inspection-and-one-manual-run. Graduation target: a contract test asserting (i) with `DEFECT_INJECTION_PYTHON` unset every `LADDER` command AND `impact_report`'s `baseline_cmd` is byte-identical to today's, guarding against a silent partial retarget; (ii) `preflight()` raises `InterpreterUnusable` on a nonexistent interpreter and `main()` converts it to exit 2 before the baseline digest and before any mutant is applied; and once P5.44 lands, (iii) `output_digest` is reproducible across two consecutive clean computations. Measured motivation: on this project's own sandbox the default `uv run` path exits 2 with "Could not acquire lock … Read-only file system", so a campaign run there before the pre-flight existed would have published ~100% detection with zero escapes. **Effort: S** | Ref: `scripts/defect_injection.py:127/150/187`
- **P5.48** [ ] **Three registered reporting portfolios have no golden — `crm-substitution`, `re-split`, `art199`** — the supervisory register's docstring delegates the "a column emptied on ONE portfolio while another still exercises the rule" case to `test_reporting_*_golden.py`, but only five exist (rich, ccr, irb-classes, off-bs, sa-classes). The three above have only focused acceptance tests pinning the columns each fixture was built for: strong there, silent elsewhere. Worst for `crm-substitution`, which the register itself calls the ONLY portfolio with a non-zero CRM-substitution cell anywhere in the estate (C 07.00 cols 0050/0060/0090/0100, C 08.01 cols 0040/0050/0070/0080, C 08.02 col 0080) — the sole route to every rule over the outflow/inflow columns, so a silent emptying takes a whole rule family dark with nothing objecting. **Effort: M** | Ref: `tests/acceptance/reporting/test_supervisory_validations.py:82-85`
- **P5.49** [ ] **Per-run `VACUOUS` is computed and gated on nothing, and it is the only metric that sees a rung-lighting improvement** — measured on task 1.4: four ERROR rules moved `VACUOUS -> PASS` (`v0320_m`, `v0326_m`, `boe_b0483`, `boe_b0490`) while `union_binding_rules_*` and `never_evaluated_rules` were **unchanged**, because none of the 36 rung-identity rules was ever NOT_EVALUATED (they were vacuous) and `union_binding_rules_*` is a union across runs so a rule already passing elsewhere is already counted. `scripts/coverage_report.py` records per-run VACUOUS in `runs[]` and aggregates it into nothing. Sibling of P5.40. Also record the measured planning fact: the rung-identity rules are `sheets_scope: "all"`, one rule per rung per regime, so lighting a rung on a second sheet buys cells but ZERO extra rules — converting more of the 36 needs more distinct RUNGS, not more portfolios. **Effort: S** | Ref: `scripts/coverage_report.py`
- **P5.50** [ ] **HIGHEST-LEVERAGE ITEM ON THE COVERAGE AXIS — an RE risk-weight portfolio in `RUNS` is the ONLY thing that can move `union_binding_rules_b31`.** Measured after the 1.4 increment: **19 of the 36 rung-identity rules are vacuous on ALL 15 emitted sheets across ALL 16 runs**, so their exposure-value column is dead everywhere and no amount of depth on existing portfolios can bind them. CRR (3): r0210 `v0323_m` 70%, r0250 `v0327_m` 250%, r0270 `v0328_m` 1250%. Basel 3.1 (16): r0160 4%, r0171 15%, r0181 25%, r0190 35%, r0191 40%, r0192 45%, r0202 65%, r0210 70%, r0221 80%, r0231 105%, r0232 110%, r0233 130%, r0234 135%, r0250 250%, r0261 400%, r0270 1250%. **Read the Basel 3.1 list and the priority orders itself**: 40/45/65% are the PS1/26 whole-loan RE LTV bands (Art. 124G / 124I) and 105/110/130/135% are the retail-and-RE currency-mismatch multiplier weights — exactly the rungs an RE risk-weight portfolio lights. So this one blocked item is simultaneously the highest-leverage remaining action. Note r0210 (70%) is likely PERMANENTLY unsatisfiable through C 07.00: no Standardised ladder in either regime contains 70%, it is an Art. 153(5) slotting weight, and slotting reports on C 08.x — so `v0323_m` / `boe_b0488` may never bind there. Related measured fact for planners: `union_binding_rules_*` is a union across runs and was ALREADY saturated for r0180 and r0240 before the 1.4 work (CRR r0180 live on 2 sheets, r0240 on 1; B31 r0180 on 3, r0240 on 1), which is why lighting them added per-portfolio depth that the union metric is structurally blind to — see P5.49. Sub-item, same portfolio:
- **P5.50a** [ ] **The OF 07.00 regulatory-real-estate of-which rows need the same new portfolio** — r0332, r0342, r0343, r0344, r0350-0354 and r0360 are confirmed dead (r0340/r0341 and r0330/r0331 are already live on `re-split`'s commercial/residential sheets). They need carriers `re-split` excludes **by documented and correct design** — `is_income_producing=False`, `is_adc=False`, `is_qualifying_re=True` on every collateral row — because a materially-dependent CRE exposure goes whole-loan under Art. 124I and so is not a split candidate. Forcing it in would break that portfolio's invariant to gain cells. All required fields exist in the schemas, so this is a fixture gap not an engine gap; the blocker is that a new portfolio must be registered in `RUNS` to be seen at all, and until it is it would DECLARE cells while lighting none — making `dead_cells` worse, which is exactly how the baseline degraded when `re-split` and `art199` were added. **Effort: M** | Ref: PS1/26 Annex II OF 07.00; `tests/fixtures/reporting_re_split_portfolio.py`

- **P5.53** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-15, batch 20260815-1, deferred half of P1.314) — `OWNER: P5.53`. No registered reporting portfolio sets `funding_currency`, so Tier 2 is structurally incapable of seeing the Art. 114(4)/115(5) funding limb move.** P1.314 added the "denominated **and funded**" limb to the direct CGCB / RGLA relief. Measured during that batch: `funding_currency` is populated in exactly ONE place in the entire test tree — `tests/fixtures/p1_229/p1_229.py:154-156`, on three loans to a GB **corporate** (the guarantor path) — so `tests/acceptance/reporting/` runs fully green under the fix **and** under no fix. P1.314 shipped the **survivor** half only: `LN_RGLA_UK` in `tests/fixtures/reporting_sa_classes_portfolio.py` was annotated `funding_currency="GBP"`, which under the permissive convention moves **zero** golden cells and proves only that the carrier reaches the reporting estate. **What is still owed is the MOVER**: a sibling GB/GBP RGLA row funded in USD, which drops from the Art. 115(5) 20% to the Art. 115(1) Table 1A weight and makes a live C 07.00 cell move. Deferred deliberately, not overlooked — adding it changes portfolio totals and therefore requires an operator-gated **surgical** golden regeneration (scoped to one `test_reporting_*_golden.py`, since `REGEN_REPORTING_GOLDENS` is read per golden file) with a per-cell preserve-or-fix record written **before** asking (LESSONS E1). Until this lands, no coverage claim for P1.314 may rest on Tier 2; the discriminating evidence is its own unit legs B/E/F/G run as two separate `-k crr` / `-k b31` invocations. **Effort: S** | Ref: CRR Art. 114(4)/(7), Art. 115(5); PS1/26 Art. 114(4), 115(2), 115(5) · Ev: `tests/fixtures/reporting_sa_classes_portfolio.py:29,307-308,442`

- **P5.51** [ ] **Two CRITICAL `python:S3776` cognitive-complexity findings in `scripts/injection_ratchet.py` — deliberately deferred, and the reasoning matters more than the fix.** SonarCloud reports complexity 36 (`check`, ~line 406) and 38 (~line 556) against an allowed 15. NOT gate-blocking: the quality gate passes and `new_maintainability_rating` is 1 (A). Left alone because **the branch count IS the specification, not accidental complexity** — `check()` has EIGHT distinct failure paths, each individually verified to exit non-zero (detected-set loss, escaped-set gain, unreachable-set gain, a gate TIMEOUT, a partial scorecard, the unreachable control coming back reachable, a known-defect escape below the floor, and a malformed `banked` status). Splitting them to satisfy a threshold risks silently dropping one, which converts a loud gate into a quiet one — the exact failure class this workstream exists to eliminate. **If taken on, do not extract by line count**: extract one NAMED predicate per failure path (`_detected_set_shrank`, `_control_became_reachable`, …), each returning a finding or `None`, held in a single explicit registry so the eight remain enumerable in one place — then assert the registry's SIZE in a test, so losing a check is a red rather than an omission. Re-verify all eight go red individually afterwards, as the original build did. Acceptable alternative: accept both in the SonarCloud UI with this reasoning, the same disposition the project already uses for its taint findings. **Effort: M (or S to accept)** | Ref: `scripts/injection_ratchet.py`; SonarCloud PR #465

- **P5.52** [ ] **The polymorphic `beneficiary_type` / `beneficiary_reference` foreign keys are still unchecked — `collateral`, `guarantees`, `provisions`** — the declaration-driven referential gate landed 2026-08-12 (`data/schemas.py::TABLE_FOREIGN_KEYS` -> `contracts/validation.py::validate_referential_integrity`, DQ005 / DQ001) and covers the five SINGLE-parent obligor links only: `loans`, `contingents`, `facilities`, `ratings`, `specialised_lending`, all pointing at `counterparties.counterparty_reference`. The three tables above were left out deliberately rather than approximated: their valid universe depends on a SECOND column (`beneficiary_type`) and spans four parent tables, so an anti-join against any one parent produces false orphans on every row addressed to a different level. The resolution already exists for the M:N table — `validate_collateral_links` / `_collateral_link_valid_beneficiaries` builds the `(lower-cased type, reference)` universe across loans / contingents / facilities / counterparties / guarantees and reports CRM010 — so this is a generalisation of working code, not new analysis: give `ForeignKey` a polymorphic variant carrying the discriminator column and the per-value parent map, and reuse that universe builder. **Measure the blast radius with the suite before landing it** (`.claude/LESSONS.md` D3): the five single-parent links cost 2 unit and 4 contract fixtures, all of which turned out to be genuinely broken bundles, and the beneficiary links reach far more fixtures. Note `tests/robustness/test_referential_integrity.py::test_an_orphan_foreign_key_accounts_for_every_row` already sweeps all nine foreign keys including these three, and PASSES — the rows survive soundly, so the triage invariant is satisfied and only the SIGNAL is missing; that test cannot be the gate for this item and a new one is needed. **Effort: M** | Ref: `src/rwa_calc/data/schemas.py::TABLE_FOREIGN_KEYS`, `contracts/validation.py::_collateral_link_valid_beneficiaries`

- **P5.17** [ ] **NEW (2026-05-28 audit — test-side companion to P8.53 + P8.39)** — No end-to-end acceptance scenario exercises the WWR LGD=100% override (P8.53) or the QCCP 2%/4% RW pin (P8.39) **through the orchestrator**. Both engine functions (`apply_wwr_gate`, `apply_ccp_risk_weight`) have thorough unit coverage (`tests/unit/ccr/test_wwr.py` 13 tests; `tests/unit/ccr/test_ccp.py` 8 tests) but those tests call the free functions directly — they do not prove the pipeline routes through them, which is exactly the gap P8.53/P8.39 describe. Track two acceptance scenarios so the implementer who wires the orchestrator call sites cannot ship without an integration pin: (a) **CCR-WWR-1** — a netting set with one `is_specific_wwr=True` trade + one non-WWR trade run through the full `PipelineOrchestrator`, asserting the WWR trade lands in a `<ns>__wwr__<trade>` synthetic NS with `wwr_lgd_override=1.0` while the non-WWR trade stays in the parent NS; (b) **CCR-CCP-1/2** — direct-cleared and client-cleared QCCP trades run end-to-end, asserting `risk_weight == 0.02` / `0.04` overrides the standard SA-Institution ladder (the load-bearing anti-degenerate: RW must NOT equal the CQS-2 institution 50%). *Formerly blocked on P8.53 / P8.39 — both closed v0.2.26, so this item is now unblocked.* **Effort: S** | Ref: PRA Rulebook CCR (CRR) Part Art. 291(5)(c), Art. 306(1); `tests/acceptance/ccr/`

#### Tier 2 — 2026-08-08 independent validation system harness gaps (PR #457, C1–C6)

> The validation harnesses landed but four capability gaps were left as deliberate follow-on commits (recorded in `docs/plans/independent-validation-system.md` and the PR). These are tooling/coverage items, not calculation defects — but until they run, the estate's ability to catch a wrong number is unmeasured. C6 in particular is the component that tells us whether any of the others work (plan §7: "if only one thing is built, build C6").

- **P5.18** [ ] **NEW (C6)** — The defect-injection scorecard exists (`scripts/defect_injection.py` + `scripts/defect_catalogue.py`) but has **never been run as a campaign**: no detection rate is measured and no nightly gate exists. Wire a nightly CI job running both `--ladder legacy` and `--ladder full` (a before/after detection rate from one build), publish the detection rate, the mean detection tier and — the load-bearing output — the list of ESCAPED mutants (changed output, every gate stayed green). Honour the three-verdict design (DETECTED / ESCAPED / UNREACHABLE) and the reachability probe so a vacuous mutant is not scored as an escape. **Effort: M** | Ref: `docs/plans/independent-validation-system.md` §C6, §7; `scripts/defect_injection.py`
- **P5.19** [ ] **NEW (C4b)** — Cell re-derivation (`tests/conformance/cell_rederivation.toml` + `test_cell_rederivation.py`) covers **OV1 only**. Extend the independently-authored cell set to **C 07.00 and C 08.01** (C 02.00 deliberately deferred — see the module docstring and `meta.limits`), so a wrong `CellSpec` carrier/scale on the SA and IRB templates is caught by a second opinion reading the same sealed ledger. **Effort: L** | Ref: `docs/plans/independent-validation-system.md` §C4b; `tests/conformance/test_cell_rederivation.py:19-25`
- **P5.20** [ ] **NEW (C3, O3)** — The oracle shadow-calculator (`tests/oracle/`) has **no CRM phase**: `drivers.py` deliberately bypasses hierarchy / classifier / CRM (docstring l.11), so no oracle exercises financial collateral, guarantees, FCCM haircuts or maturity mismatch. Build a CRM-stage driver and the ~25 O3 oracles the plan scopes (Art. 222/224/230/233/238 territory), each hand-derived and independent of the engine (the `test_derivations_never_import_rwa_calc` lock still applies). **Effort: L** | Ref: `docs/plans/independent-validation-system.md` §C3 (O3, ~25 exposures); `tests/oracle/drivers.py`
- **P5.22** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-08, batch 20260808-1624)** — **`cp_sovereign_cqs` is dead in the supervisory register.** Measured: **no** reporting portfolio module (`tests/fixtures/reporting_*.py`) sets `sovereign_cqs` at all, so the column is null in every `RUNS` entry and every rule over it is `NOT_EVALUATED` — indistinguishable from clean. That was tolerable while the column only fed a floor; **P1.316 would make it drive the institution risk weight itself** (Art. 121(1) Table 5), so the blind spot becomes load-bearing. Give one reporting portfolio an unrated institution with a non-null `sovereign_cqs` and register it. Note the same batch proved the value of doing this: adding two rows to `reporting_irb_classes_portfolio` moved **five** rules from `VACUOUS` to `PASS`, including `boe_b0752_27`, the C 08.01 r0253 tie-out. **Effort: S** | Ref: LESSONS B5 (fail-open coverage)
- **P5.21** [ ] **NEW (C5)** — Coverage-as-a-ratchet is computed but **not gated**. `scripts/coverage_report.py` prints `union_binding_rules_crr/b31`, `template_cell_liveness_bp`, `dead_cells` and `never_evaluated_rules`, and `scripts/coverage_baseline.json` records a baseline, but these metrics are absent from `scripts/arch_metrics.json` and there is no `--check` gate or CI wiring (the script itself says so at `:45-46`: "does not gate … deliberately somebody else's commit"). Add the four metrics to `arch_metrics.json` with directional ratchets (binding-rule counts and cell liveness may not decrease; `dead_cells` may not increase) and a `--check` mode invoked in CI, so a new blind spot fails the gate. **Effort: M** | Ref: `docs/plans/independent-validation-system.md` §C5; `scripts/coverage_report.py:45-46`, `scripts/coverage_baseline.json`

Please use implode to return to previous behavior."` (observed on every CCR acceptance run, e.g. emitted from `engine/materialise.py:146` collect path). Representative sites: `reporting/corep/generator.py:2300,2348,2628,3980,4133,4156,4164,4166,4180`, `engine/classifier.py:313,316,1841,1910`, `engine/hierarchy.py:2494,2779`, `engine/equity/calculator.py:551`, `engine/ccf.py:300`. The warning is currently benign but a future Polars major will change `is_in([...])` semantics or remove the list-collection form. Sweep all 70 sites to the recommended form (wrap the literal in `pl.Series` / use `.implode()` per the polars guidance, or pin the comparison dtype). Pure forward-compat / code-hygiene — no current calculation or test impact (warnings only). **Effort: M** | Ref: https://github.com/pola-rs/polars/issues/22149; `grep -rn "\\.is_in(\\[" src/rwa_calc/` (70 hits)

- **P6.47** [ ] **NEW (2026-07-07 — durable root-cause fix behind the reconciliation loan-detail 500)** — `ResultsCache` writes every run to a **fixed** `<cache_dir>/last_results.parquet` (`src/rwa_calc/api/results_cache.py:130`), so a later `CreditRiskCalc.calculate()` / `.reconcile()` overwrites the very file that a still-registered `ReconciliationResponse` / `CalculationResponse` re-scans lazily on demand — a torn / stale read raising `polars ComputeError: parquet: File out of specification: The page header reported the wrong page size`. The Unreleased fix mitigated it two ways (reconciliation `loan_detail` + CSV/Excel exports now filter the **memoised** snapshot instead of re-scanning disk; all `ResultsCache` writes are now atomic via temp-file + `os.replace`), but the fixed-filename hazard remains for **any** consumer that re-scans on demand — results-page pagination (`get_page` / `scan_results`), a reconcile `component_reconciliation` / `class_allocation` re-scan, or a future export. Durable fix: give each run a **unique** results filename (`results_<run_id>.parquet`) so every registered response scans an **immutable snapshot**, and re-point `load_cached()` (which currently keys on the fixed name, `:163-187`) at a "latest" pointer — a small manifest JSON, or an atomically-updated copy/symlink. **Caveat:** needs a retention/GC policy (ref-count or TTL) so a snapshot is never deleted while a live response still references it. **Effort: M** | Ref: `src/rwa_calc/api/results_cache.py:130,163-187`, `src/rwa_calc/api/service.py:96-103,227`; `docs/appendix/changelog.md` [Unreleased] → Fixed

#### Tier 6 — 2026-07-19 reporting-estate architecture review

> Six deliberate follow-ups from a 2026-07-19 architecture review of the COREP/Pillar III reporting estate (post-Phase-7 declarative-reporting completion — see the Architecture Migration cross-reference above). Not audit findings — the generators are functionally correct today; these are structural debt items surfaced by the review. Spot-checked against source: `reporting/corep/generator.py` (902 lines) and `reporting/pillar3/generator.py` (1012 lines) confirmed near-identical shape; `reporting/catalog.py::_TEMPLATES` confirmed as a 33-entry hand-maintained tuple (`catalog.py:132-178`); `reporting/corep/c08.py` confirmed 1482 lines; `_const`/`_Row`/`_null_empty_rows` duplication across c07/c08/c09 confirmed by grep; `Formula(..., fn=_const)` confirmed at 43 of 68 total `Formula(...)` call sites in `reporting/`; C34.01/02/04/08 and CCR1/2/3/8 confirmed present only as catalogue/column-list entries (`catalog.py`, `corep/templates.py`, `pillar3/templates.py`) with no per-template `cellspec` module (unlike c07/c08/c09/cr4-cr10/cms1/cms2/ov1, which each have one); `tests/fixtures/recon_ledger.py::with_reporting_ledger()` confirmed as a hand-listed column mapping independent of `contracts/edges.py::AGGREGATOR_EXIT_EDGE.conform()`; `api/rest.py` confirmed module-level `_RUNS`/`_TEMPLATE_BUNDLES` dicts (`:66,113`) with no persistence.

- **P6.48** [ ] **NEW (2026-07-19 architecture review)** **Registry-driven unified `TemplateGenerator`** — replace `COREPGenerator` + `Pillar3Generator` (two ~1000-line files with identical shape: frozen bundle, `generate`/`generate_from_lazyframe`/`export_to_excel` trio, per-field dispatch, sheet-writer helpers) with one generator driven by a template registry (id, family, framework-variant column resolver, spec-builder-or-imperative callable, axis kind, sheet naming). Bundle becomes id-keyed. Registry sourced from the cited rulepack `ReportingTemplateSet` (`resolve().reporting`; `rulebook/resolve.py`, `rulebook/model.py`), retiring the hand-maintained 33-entry `catalog.py::_TEMPLATES` list and closing the pack-blind gap noted in `reporting/metadata.py`. Export loop becomes data-driven off the registry. Number-neutral; goldens guard. **Sequence before any new template family is added.** High effort, high impact. **Effort: L** | Ref: `reporting/corep/generator.py`, `reporting/pillar3/generator.py`, `reporting/catalog.py:132-178`, `reporting/metadata.py`
- **P6.49** [ ] **NEW (2026-07-19 architecture review)** **Kernel-ise shared template helpers + two DSL primitives** — lift the copy-pasted `_prepare` discriminator ladders (defaulted/substitution/on-off-BS in c07/c08 and partially c09 — verify each ladder is identical before merging; keep c09's divergence explicit), `_null_empty_rows`, `_negate`/`_negate_deduction_cols`, `_const` (verbatim in `c07.py`/`c08.py`/`c09.py`), `_Row` (`c08.py`/`c09.py`), and the CR6/CR9 PD-band alloc+label+empty-band pattern into `reporting/kernel/`. Add a first-class `Constant` verb to `reporting/cellspec.py` (retires the `Formula(refs=(), fn=_const)` hack — confirmed 43 of 68 `Formula(...)` sites in `reporting/`, i.e. the majority) and a data-driven-row primitive (retires `_Row`). Also fix the `Formula` docstring's stale "~5 cells" claim in `cellspec.py`. Number-neutral. **Effort: M** | Ref: `reporting/corep/c07.py`, `reporting/corep/c08.py`, `reporting/corep/c09.py`, `reporting/cellspec.py`
- **P6.50** [ ] **NEW (2026-07-19 architecture review)** **Migrate C34.01/02/04/08 (COREP) and CCR1/2/3/8 (Pillar 3) onto the cellspec executor** — goldens FIRST per the recorded S8-pre rule (don't strangle an un-goldened template): author golden fixtures for each family, then port the imperative catalogue/column-list blocks (`reporting/catalog.py`, `reporting/corep/templates.py`, `reporting/pillar3/templates.py` — confirmed these 8 templates have no per-template `cellspec` module today, unlike c07/c08/c09/cr4-cr10/cms1/cms2/ov1) to per-template spec modules. This extends cell lineage (currently instrumented on a minority of the 33 catalogued templates) and any tie-out layer to those templates. **Depends on P6.49** — do not multiply existing duplication by porting against the un-kernelised helpers. **Effort: L** | Ref: `reporting/catalog.py:149-158,174-177`, `reporting/corep/templates.py`, `reporting/pillar3/templates.py`
- **P6.51** [ ] **NEW (2026-07-19 architecture review)** **Split `corep/c08.py` per sub-template** — 1482 lines / confirmed 107 `CellSpec(` sites covering C08.01-07 with materially different row logic; pure file split to `c08_01.py`..`c08_07.py` modules matching the one-module-per-template pattern used everywhere else in `reporting/corep/` and `reporting/pillar3/`. No logic change; import-path churn in tests and the `recon_ledger` shim. **Effort: M** | Ref: `reporting/corep/c08.py` (1482 lines, 107 `CellSpec(` sites)
- **P6.52** [ ] **NEW (2026-07-19 architecture review)** **LedgerShim conform via the real edge contract** — `tests/fixtures/recon_ledger.py::with_reporting_ledger()` hand-lists the `reporting_*` columns it injects onto synthetic unit frames (`:34-58`) and can drift from `contracts/edges.py::AGGREGATOR_EXIT_EDGE`; have the shim run frames through `AGGREGATOR_EXIT_EDGE.conform()` so fixtures track the declared schema automatically (failures surface real gaps — that's the point). **Effort: S** | Ref: `tests/fixtures/recon_ledger.py:34-58`, `src/rwa_calc/contracts/edges.py`

### Tier 8 — Counterparty Credit Risk (CCR) Integration (NEW 2026-05-21; reordered 2026-05-22 for first-batch execution)

**Status (2026-07-21 housekeeping):** the engine phases of this epic are **complete** and pruned to the Completed Items list — foundations + CCR-A1 (v0.2.11), the orchestrator-wiring quartet P8.19/P8.39/P8.53/P8.54, SA-CCR formula coverage (P8.15), CCR→IRB routing (P8.31), the CCR PD floor (P8.32), default-fund capital (P8.49), reporting (P8.50–P8.52), output floor (P8.55), BA-CVA (P8.60/P8.62/P8.63), and the acceptance-scenario families P8.42–P8.45. **Remaining:** P8.26 (nominally superseded by the now-closed P8.49 — verify no residual scope, then close), the test/citation residuals P8.46–P8.48, documentation P8.70–P8.74, and the Phase 10 v2.0 deferrals.

Major new epic adding Counterparty Credit Risk to the project. CCR is a separate-but-adjacent risk type to default credit risk: it produces the **EAD** for derivative and SFT (securities financing transaction) exposures, which then flows through the existing SA / IRB risk-weight machinery to produce the counterparty default-risk RWA. CVA (Credit Valuation Adjustment) capital is a related but separate framework and is scoped as a sub-epic (P8.60–P8.69).

**Pipeline shape:** the CCR stage sits between `Loader` and `Classifier` (or between `HierarchyResolver` and `Classifier`, TBD per P8.20). It consumes trade-level and netting-set-level inputs, produces exposure-level rows keyed by counterparty, and merges those rows into the existing `ExposureBundle` so downstream SA / IRB / Aggregator stages need minimal change.

**Execution ordering (2026-05-22 operator-approved):** the **first executable batch** (top of this tier) ships Phase 1 foundations + a single end-to-end vertical slice — **CCR-A1** (one unmargined IR swap → SA-CCR EAD → SA-RW → aggregator → pinned acceptance test). Source plan: `.claude/plans/can-we-start-to-linear-steele.md`. Within Tier 8 the first-batch items are listed at the top in dependency order; items marked **deferred to next batch after CCR-A1** are listed below them; items marked **deferred to v2.0** sit in Phase 10 alongside IMM.

**Scope decisions (operator-confirmed 2026-05-22):**
1. **Simplified SA-CCR (Art. 281) and OEM (Art. 282) — deferred to v2.0 / next-major-epic.** Not in initial epic. Sub-threshold portfolios fall through to full SA-CCR (always conservative). P8.21 and P8.22 reclassified accordingly (moved to Phase 10).
2. **CVA — BA-CVA only initially.** P8.60 (BA-CVA), P8.62 (hedges eligibility), P8.63 (aggregated CVA on bundle) stay in scope as the CVA sub-epic. **P8.61 (SA-CVA) deferred to v2.0** (moved to Phase 10).
3. **IMM — confirmed deferred to v2.0** (no change; P8.90 / P8.91 already there).
4. **SFTs — route through CCR with `risk_type=CCR_SFT`, reuse existing CRM haircut tables in `engine/crm/`.** No duplication of the supervisory haircut table. ~~The dual `risk_type` routing is already encompassed by existing tickets (no new P-code).~~ **Correction (2026-05-28):** the closing sentence proved wrong in practice. The current `engine/ccr/sa_ccr.py::compute_ead` runs the derivative `α·(RC+PFE)` formula on `transaction_type="sft"` rows regardless — only `engine/ccr/maturity_factor.py:121-127` branches on the SFT flag (5-BD margined MF floor). For a £60.7M debt-security SFT booked through the `trades` table this gives ~£4M EAD instead of the ~£65M `E·(1+HE)` the FCCM formula would produce. SFT-specific EAD wiring under SA-CCR per CRR Art. 271(2) was tracked under **P8.38** (since closed — the FCCM SFT branch shipped and was promoted to `engine/sft/fccm.py`).

**Cross-cutting blockers:**
- Trade-level fixtures are a step-change from the current exposure-level parquet shape. `fixture-builder` agent needs new builders before any acceptance scenario can be pinned (P8.40).
- The `output_floor.py` numerator must include SA-CCR-derived EAD × SA-RW for IMM firms (P8.55).

#### Phase 3 — Alternative methods, CCP, and edge cases (in scope of epic; later batches)

- **P8.26** [ ] **SUPERSEDED BY P8.49 (2026-05-28)** — Default fund contributions to QCCP. `engine/ccr/ccp.py::compute_dfc_capital`. Per Art. 308 alternative formula `K_CCP × DF_i / DF_CM`. Non-QCCP default fund contributions: 1,250% RW (Art. 309). P8.49 below carries the load-bearing description (new `default_fund.py` module, schema columns, data-table constants, acceptance scenarios CCR-B2..B4). Note: the original P8.26 reference to "1,250% RW (Art. 309)" was a simplification — Art. 309(2) actually applies the 12.5× multiplier to the K capital requirement (same mechanism as Art. 308(3) for QCCPs), not a flat 1,250% RW on the contribution amount. **Effort: M** | Ref: CRR Art. 308, 309 — see P8.49

The bullet list above is the canonical work-queue. The items below have substantive sub-issue structure that doesn't compress cleanly into a single bullet, so they are kept in long-form.

### Tier 9 — CRR / Basel 3.1 Conformance Audit (workflow 2026-06-01)

Generated by the regulatory-conformance-audit workflow on 2026-06-01; each item independently reviewer-confirmed. Promote capital-impacting items into Tier 1 on the next curator pass.

#### Capital-impacting

- **P1.198** [ ] **DISPUTED — likely false-positive audit finding; do NOT implement without authoritative PS1/26 PDF confirmation (investigated batch 20260601-2211, operator-confirmed drop).** The plan premise (PS1/26 Art. 230(1)(b)/231 adds an 8% Hfx on **funded non-financial** FCM collateral when currencies differ) directly contradicts the SSOT spec `docs/specifications/crr/credit-risk-mitigation.md:257-260` and the `basel31` skill, which both state `HFX = 0` for funded non-financial collateral under BOTH CRR and B31 (the 8% Hfx applies only to **financial** collateral via Art. 224 FCCM, and to **unfunded** protection via Art. 233(3) — the latter is P1.200's territory; currency mismatch on non-financial collateral is handled by upstream spot rebasing in `FXConverter`). The conformance-audit appears to have conflated the Art. 233 unfunded-protection FX haircut with funded non-financial collateral. Implementing the "fix" would INTRODUCE an erroneous over-charge. The CRR side is already correct (HFX=0). **Re-open only if a direct read of `ps126app1.pdf` Art. 230(1)(b)/231 verbatim text confirms a non-financial Hfx; otherwise close as not-a-bug.** ~~**B31 FCM LGD* omits the Hfx currency-mismatch haircut on non-financial collateral (Art. 230(1)(b))**~~ — `crm/haircuts.py:221-229` gates the FX haircut to financial collateral only (`is_financial = ~collateral_type.is_in(NON_FINANCIAL_COLLATERAL_TYPES)`), but PS1/26 Art. 230(1)(b)/231 Ci(b) require an explicit Hfx (8% @10-day) on ES for receivables/immovable/other physical when currencies differ. The missing term flows `value_after_haircut` → `effectively_secured` (`collateral.py:638-641`) → `lgd_star_expr` (`collateral.py:1052-1061`), overstating the secured portion. Fix must be `is_basel_3_1`-conditional (keep financial-only for CRR; allow non-financial Hfx under B31). Constant `schemas.py:1275`. **Effort: M** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 230(1)(b), 231 Ci(b)/231(2)(a) (ps126app1.pdf PDF idx 208-210); contrast CRR Art. 230 (crr.pdf pp.226-227, no Hfx)
- **P1.199** [ ] **CIU/fund units as collateral mis-bucket to other_physical (flat 40% HC) instead of Art. 224(5) look-through volatility adjustment** — `crm/haircuts.py:592-616` (`_normalize_collateral_type_expr`) has no CIU/fund branch, so eligible CIU collateral (Art. 197(5)/(6)) falls to `.otherwise(other_physical)` and the financial-haircut join misses → `:576 .fill_null(0.40)`. CIU also absent from `COLLATERAL_TYPE_CATEGORY` (`schemas.py:1252`), giving other_physical LGDS (40% CRR / 25% B31, `crm_supervisory.py:53,67`) not financial 0%. Recognise eligible CIU as financial with a volatility adjustment = weighted-average of underlying-asset haircuts (or highest where holdings unknown) per Art. 224(5); no Art. 224(5) collateral look-through helper exists in `engine/crm/` (the `equity/calculator.py` CIU code is the unrelated Art. 132 equity path). **Effort: L** | Ref: CRR Art. 224(5) (crr.pdf p.221), Art. 197(5)-(6) (crr.pdf p.194); B31/FCCM retention via comparison PDF p.28
- **P1.201** [ ] **Art. 233(2)(b) 60%-of-exposure cap missing for over-covering no-restructuring CDS** — `crm/guarantees.py:1077-1086` (`_apply_restructuring_haircut_to_guarantees`) applies `amount_covered*(1-0.40)` for both limbs of Art. 233(2) but never caps G* at 60% of exposure value when G > E. For G=200, EAD=100 the only downstream cap (cap-at-EAD pro-rata at `:627-631`) yields recognised protection=100 instead of 0.60×100=60 — over-recognition up to 40% of EAD, anti-conservative. Apply `min(amount_covered, 0.60*exposure_value)` for the no-restructuring branch when `amount_covered > exposure_value`. The unused twin `_apply_restructuring_exclusion_haircut` (`:1313-1365`) shares the defect. Closely related to P1.202 (same function). **Effort: S** | Ref: CRR Art. 233(2)(a)/(b) (crr.pdf p.230); PS1/26 Art. 233(2) (no override)
- **P1.202** [ ] **Restructuring-exclusion 40% haircut skips `percentage_covered` credit derivatives** — `crm/guarantees.py:1071-1086` (`_apply_restructuring_haircut_to_guarantees`, called `:193`) gates on `amount_covered>0` and rewrites only `amount_covered`, so a no-restructuring CDS supplied via `percentage_covered` (amount null/0) gets no 40% reduction and is recognised at full G (resolved `:893`). Mirror the FX sibling's percentage branch (`:1026-1033`): add a `percentage_covered` output expr and broaden the gate to `has_coverage` (amount OR percentage>0). Dead correct-shape variant at `:1313`. Add an acceptance test for the percentage-CDS restructuring path. Same function as P1.201. **Effort: S** | Ref: CRR Art. 233(2)(a)-(b), 233(1), 216 (crr.pdf pp.~229-230); PS1/26 carries Art. 233(2)
- **P1.203** [ ] **Double-default eligibility admits ineligible guarantor classes (sovereign/MDB/corporate) and omits Art. 202(b)-(d) CQS/PD gates** — `irb/guarantee.py:622-629` (`_apply_double_default`, `_is_eligible_guarantor_type`) flags ['institution','mdb','central_govt_central_bank'] as Art. 153(3) DD-eligible with no CQS/PD gate, plus corporates at CQS≤2. Art. 202 limits eligible DD providers to institutions, investment firms, insurers/reinsurers and ECAs meeting (a) expertise, (b) regulated-equivalent OR ECAI CQS≤3, (c) internal PD≤CQS2, (d) internal PD≤CQS3 — sovereigns, MDBs, corporates are NOT eligible. Restrict to institution (+investment_firm/insurer/eca if modelled), drop govt/mdb/corporate, apply CQS≤3 + internal-PD gates. CRR-only, gated behind `config.enable_double_default` (default False). **Effort: M** | Ref: CRR Art. 202(a)-(d) (crr.pdf p.201)
- **P1.204** [ ] **B31 slotting col A/C short-maturity discount applied mandatorily; Art. 153(5)(d) makes it an optional "may" election over the B/D default** — the B31 branch of `lookup_rw` (`slotting/transforms.py` — *paths refreshed 2026-07-07: was `slotting/namespace.py:377-387`, retired Phase 4*) unconditionally selects the col-A/C short tables (pack `slotting_rw_short` / `slotting_rw_hvcre` in `rulebook/packs/b31.py` — *was `b31_slotting.py`, moved to packs Phase 5*; non-HVCRE 50/70; HVCRE 70/95) whenever `is_short_maturity`, with no opt-in. Art. 153(5)(c) makes col B/D (70/90; 95/120) the mandatory default and 153(5)(d) makes col A/C a firm election, so the engine understates RWA vs the default for short-dated Strong/Good SL. Add an election flag (input column or `CalculationConfig` field, mirroring `look_through_election`) gating col A/C, defaulting to B/D; CRR path stays auto. Also fix stale "Not Yet Implemented" at `docs/specifications/basel31/slotting-approach.md:249-267` (docs-side — see cross-file note). `is_short` derivation now in `slotting/transforms.py`. **Effort: M** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 153(5)(c)-(d) Table A (ps126app1.pdf pp.102-103); contrast CRR Art. 153(5) Table 1 (crr.pdf p.150)
- **P1.205** [ ] **CRR Art. 165(1)(a)/(b) 0.09% equity PD floors defined but unreachable — no input column to select long-term-relationship / regular-cashflow sub-types** — `equity/calculator.py:857-869` (PD/LGD branch) reads only `EQUITY_PD_FLOORS['exchange_traded']` (0.40%) and `['other']` (1.25%); `pd_floored` has two outcomes. The two 0.09% keys `exchange_traded_long_term` / `non_exchange_regular_cashflow` (`crr_equity_pd_lgd.py:38-39`) have zero consumers and no input column selects them (`VALID_EQUITY_TYPES` `schemas.py:1304-1316`, `EQUITY_EXPOSURES` `:605-629`). Result: exchange-traded long-term floored at 0.40% not 0.09% (165(1)(a)); non-exchange cashflow-based at 1.25% not 0.09% (165(1)(b)). Conservative but diverges. Add an equity sub-type flag + four-way `pd_floored`; add CRR acceptance tests. **Effort: M** | Ref: CRR Art. 165(1)(a)-(d) (crr.pdf p.164)
- **P1.206** [ ] **PD/LGD equity LGD (Art. 165(2)) keys 65% diversified-PE on type-string only, ignoring `is_diversified_portfolio` the IRB-Simple branch honours** — `equity/calculator.py:871-876` assigns 65% only when `equity_type=='private_equity_diversified'`, whereas the IRB-Simple branch at `:731-738` also recognises `equity_type=='private_equity' & is_diversified_portfolio==True`. No step normalises the flag into the type string. An identical diversified PE exposure encoded via the boolean gets 90% LGD under PD/LGD but the diversified treatment under IRB-Simple — inconsistent Art. 165(2). Broaden the `:872` `when` to OR in `(eq_type=='private_equity') & is_diversified_portfolio.fill_null(False)`. **Effort: S** | Ref: CRR Art. 165(2) (crr.pdf p.164; gated by Art. 155(3))
- **P1.207** [ ] **B31 `is_speculative` flag short-circuits to 400% bypassing the unlisted-AND-business<5y higher-risk test (Art. 133(4))** — `equity/calculator.py:692-695` (`_apply_b31_equity_weights_sa`) assigns 400% whenever `is_speculative==True` OR `equity_type=='speculative'`, evaluated independent of `is_unlisted` (`:665`) and `business_age` (`:670-680`), so a LISTED or ≥5y-business equity flagged speculative wrongly gets 400% instead of 250% (Art. 133(3)). PS1/26 glossary defines higher-risk equity solely as unlisted AND business<5y — no speculative tier. Same ungated OR repeats in the transitional floor at `:968+991`. Feed `is_speculative` into the unlisted/business-age determination rather than letting it force 400%, in both branches. **Effort: S** | Ref: PS1/26 Glossary "higher risk equity exposure" (ps126app1.pdf p.5), Art. 133(3)/(4)
- **P1.208** [ ] **SA provision deduction sums all provision rows — GCRA wrongly reduce SA exposure value (Art. 111(1) is SCRA-only)** — `crm/provisions.py:166-273` (`_resolve_provisions_multi_level`) aggregates `pl.col('amount')` across all PROVISION_SCHEMA rows into `provision_allocated` with no `provision_type` filter; the SA drawn-first branch (`:92-117`) deducts the entire `provision_allocated` from exposure value. CRR Art. 111(1) reduces SA exposure by SPECIFIC CRA only — general CRA flows to Tier 2 via Art. 110(1)/62(c). Filter the SA deduction to `provision_type=='scra'` (null/legacy as scra), exclude `gcra` and route it to the unused `gcra_provision_amount` output (`schemas.py:2054`) / Tier 2 path. Shares the no-`provision_type`-split root cause with P1.210. **Effort: M** | Ref: CRR Art. 111(1) with Art. 110(1)/62(c) (crr.pdf pp.109-110); PS1/26 Art. 111 unchanged — *2026-07-07 compliance audit re-confirmed (art111-gcra-included-in-sa-provision-deduction, §6).*
- **P1.209** [ ] **Art. 158(6A) post-model EL gross-up bypasses the Art. 159 shortfall/excess test (`el_after_adjustment` ignored)** — `irb/adjustments.py:255-264` (`apply_post_model_adjustments`) writes the grossed-up EL to `el_after_adjustment` but leaves `expected_loss` unchanged; `compute_el_shortfall_excess` (`:344`, called at `calculator.py:242` right after PMA at `:241`) reads the pre-PMA `expected_loss`, so `el_shortfall/el_excess` (summed in `_el_summary.py:100-105`) ignore the mandatory EL increase. Feed `el_after_adjustment` into `compute_el_shortfall_excess` on the B31 path. Latent until a non-zero `pma_el_scalar` is configured (B31 default 0.0, `config.py:540/574`); when set it understates the CET1 shortfall / overstates T2 excess. **Effort: S** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 158(6A) with Art. 159(1), 146(3)(c) (provisions.md:93-102,250,362-364)
- **P1.210** [ ] **Art. 159(3) defaulted pool (Pool D) wrongly includes GCRA — `provision_type` never split GCRA/SCRA upstream** — `crm/provisions.py:_resolve_provisions_multi_level` (`:169/200/239/270`) sums provision amounts with no `provision_type` filter, so `provision_allocated = GCRA+SCRA` undifferentiated; `irb/adjustments.py:362,367` builds per-exposure `el_excess` from this combined pool_b — for a defaulted obligor any attached GCRA inflates `el_excess`. `_el_summary.py:200-201` sums into `d_excess`; the Art. 159(3) split branch (`:217,226`) sets `effective_excess = d_excess` feeding `t2_credit` (`:237`). Pool D is SPECIFIC-CRA-on-defaulted only; general CRA must go to non-defaulted Pool B. Split `provision_allocated` by `provision_type` so GCRA always lands in pool_b. Shares root cause with P1.208. **Effort: M** | Ref: CRR Art. 159(3) (provisions.md:154-159,179-198); PS1/26 retained Art. 159
- **P1.211** [ ] **Infrastructure supporting factor (0.75) lacks corporate/SL class gate AND defaulted-exposure exclusion (Art. 501a(1)(a))** *(merged: this item subsumes the narrower defaulted-only finding)* — `supporting_factors.py:422-429` (`apply_factors`, `infra_factor_expr`) and `:174` (`get_effective_factor`) apply the 0.75 factor gated solely on `is_infrastructure`, which `classifier.py:758` derives only from `product_type` containing 'INFRASTRUCTURE' (no class condition, survives default rerouting at `:748-758`). Art. 501a(1)(a) requires the exposure be in corporate OR specialised-lending class AND excludes defaulted exposures. Gate so 0.75 applies only when `exposure_class` is CORPORATE/SPECIALISED_LENDING and `~is_defaulted` (the SME branch already excludes defaults at `:387/406`); else 1.0. Add a defaulted-infrastructure regression test. **Effort: S** | Ref: CRR Art. 501a(1)(a) (crr.pdf PDF idx 418)
- **P1.212** [ ] **GCRA cap base uses floor-eligible S-TREA, not full standardised TREA (Art. 92(3A))** — `aggregator/aggregator.py:185-192` computes the GCRA cap base (`s_trea_pre`) by filtering `combined` to FLOOR_ELIGIBLE_APPROACHES (IRB+slotting only), excluding pure-SA and equity rows present in the frame. Art. 92(2A) caps GCRA at 1.25% of paragraph-3A S-TREA = SA-equivalent RWA across ALL business activities, not just the modelled subset. Sum `sa_rwa` over FLOOR_ELIGIBLE + SA + EQUITY approaches (whole `combined` frame) before `compute_of_adj` (`:196`); cap applied at `_floor.py:89-90`. For mixed firms the too-small base over-tightens the cap, inflating OF-ADJ (over-conservative). Add a mixed-approach test (`test_of_adj.py` uses IRB-only). **Effort: S** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 92(2A), 92(3A), 92(3)(a) (ps126app1.pdf pp.13,15)
- **P1.213** [ ] **SA-CCR option supervisory delta uses original tenor for Black-Scholes T instead of remaining time-to-expiry from reporting_date** — `ccr/supervisory_delta.py:136-138` sets `t_years=(maturity_date-start_date)/365` and feeds d1 (`:144`); the signature (`:85`) takes no `reporting_date`. For any seasoned option (reporting_date>start_date) T is overstated, so d1, Φ(d1), delta, PFE add-on and EAD are wrong. Thread `reporting_date` in and compute `T=max((maturity_date-reporting_date)/yearfrac, 0)`, reusing the reporting-relative `years_to_maturity` at `pipeline_adapter.py:245-246` (called `:269`), matching the S/E convention in `adjusted_notional.py:90-91`. **Effort: S** | Ref: BCBS CRE52.42 / CRE52.40; CRR Art. 279a(2) (onshored under SA-CCR / PS1/26 CRR Part)

#### Reporting & docs

- **P2.50** [ ] **B31 IRB retail PD floor: 0.10% applied to all residential-mortgage retail; Art. 163(1)(b) limits 0.10% to UK-located RRE, non-UK RRE retail should floor at 0.05% (Art. 163(1)(c))** — `irb/formulas.py:128` (`_pd_floor_expression`) routes every `exposure_class` matching 'MORTGAGE'|'RESIDENTIAL' to `floors.retail_mortgage=0.0010` (`config.py:109`) with no property-location gating (`namespace.py:543` calls it). PS1/26 Art. 163(1)(b) restricts 0.10% to retail secured by RRE "located in the UK"; (c) puts all-other-retail (incl. non-UK RRE) at 0.05%. No property-country column exists, so a fix needs a new UK-property input flag + a gated branch + a `retail_mortgage_non_uk`=0.0005 floor. Divergence is conservative (overstates capital), edge case for UK retail. *(confidence: medium)* **Effort: M** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 163(1)(b)/(c) (ps126app1.pdf PDF idx 114)
- **P2.51** [ ] **CIU equity-underlying Rule 4.8(3) floor uses a flat 370% instead of the underlying's per-type Art. 155(2) weight (190/290/370)** — `equity/calculator.py:552-553` (`_equity_holding_higher_of_rw`) returns `max(_IRB_RW[OTHER]=370%, transitional_rw)` as a single scalar applied via `pl.lit()` (`:439`) to ALL equity-class CIU look-through underlyings. Rule 4.8(3) requires the per-underlying Art. 155(2) weight: 190% diversified PE / 290% exchange-traded-listed / 370% other. Over-states RW for ET/diversified-PE underlyings (over-capital). Needs `CIU_HOLDINGS_SCHEMA` (`schemas.py:647-653`) extended with the underlying equity-type/listing/diversification field, plumbed through `_resolve_look_through_rw` (`:434-441`), then branch the floor per underlying. **Effort: M** | Ref: PS1/26 App 1 Rule 4.8(3) (ps126app1.pdf pp.21-22); CRR Art. 155(2) 190/290/370 (`crr_equity_rw.py:57-72`)

### Closed as not-a-defect — DO NOT RE-FILE

- **P1.305** (2026-08-13) — **"the LGD side is missing the PD side's corporate gate"**. It is not missing it; it must not have one. **The prescribed gate is RWA-REDUCING by up to 60pp.** Retail purchased receivables have their **own** LGD rule: CRR Art. 164(1) *"For dilution risk of purchased receivables, an **LGD value of 75%** shall be used"*, and PS1/26 Art. 164(1)(b) *"for dilution risk of purchased receivables, use an **LGD value of 100%**"*. The engine's ungated branch already returns exactly those — measured 0.75 (CRR) / 1.00 (B31) on a retail A-IRB dilution row — so it is right **by construction in both regimes**, not by coincidence in one. Adding the class gate drops those rows through `.otherwise(seniority_based_lgd_expr)` to the generic senior-unsecured default: retail dilution **0.75 → 0.45 (−30pp) under CRR** and **1.00 → 0.40 (−60pp) under B31**, retail subordinated 1.00 → 0.75. So "conservative in effect" is backwards — the current state is exactly right for the one retail purchased-receivable population the articles legislate for. **The asymmetry with the PD side is deliberate and already documented:** the P1.278 gate's own docstring (`stages/classify/subtypes.py:385-394`) explains that Art. 160(2)/(6) name the *corporate* population and the denominator is a *corporate* supervisory LGD, so a retail row dividing EL by it would manufacture an unauthorised PD — reasoning that is sound for the PD side and does not transfer to the LGD side, where Art. 164(1) supplies a retail rate of its own. `engine/irb/transforms.py:223-225` separately records rejecting the *approach*-gate variant of the same mistake for the same anti-conservative reason. The bullet's "such a row is already malformed" is also false for the case that matters: Art. 164(1) *prescribes* a supervisory LGD with no own estimate, so a retail dilution row with a null `lgd` is precisely the shape the article contemplates. **Sequencing note for P1.348:** its defect currently suppresses this population (an A-IRB row with null `lgd` is demoted to F-IRB/SA before reaching the transform), so **fixing P1.348 will create it** — whoever lands it must assert the retail dilution cell holds at 0.75 / 1.00 rather than dropping to the generic default.

Each was investigated to a verbatim reading of the primary text and deliberately not implemented. The reasoning is the deliverable; re-filing any of them repeats work already paid for. Full prose in `git log -p IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` at the dates below.

- **P1.284** (2026-07-26) — CRR Art. 119(2)/(3) short-term national-currency institution relief. Deliberate conservative simplification: the repo expresses exactly one Art. 114(4)/(7) preferential value, so "one category less favourable" collapses to the Art. 119(3) 20% floor; the relief is conditioned on *funding* currency and the only funding-currency helper is null-permissive, so a reuse would grant new relief on denomination alone. Omission over-states only, and PS1/26 marks Art. 119(2)/(3)/(4) "[Note: Provision left blank]". Reopen only for a material ≤3m book to CQS4-6 institutions in their own jurisdiction's currency with `funding_currency` populated.
- **P1.285** (2026-07-26) — CRR Art. 119(4) minimum reserves held via an intermediary. A permissive firm election whose conditions are legal attestations with no schema carrier, and it is **already reachable** by booking the reserve against a `central_bank` counterparty (`cp_country_code="GB"`, GBP), which yields the same 0%. Over-states only. PS1/26 leaves the paragraph blank.
- **P1.286** (2026-07-24) — **REFUTED.** The bullet read PS1/26's `[Note: Provision not in PRA Rulebook]` on Art. 119(5)-(6) as deletion. It is not: "[Note: Provision left blank]" means deleted, "[Note: Provision not in PRA Rulebook]" means it survives in CRR. PS1/26 cross-references "Article 119(5) of CRR" seven times and builds the IRB institutions class and CRM eligibility on it, so `financial_institution` → INSTITUTION is correct under **both** regimes. Implementing it would also have been anti-conservative (−50pp on a null `cp_scra_grade`).
- **P1.287** (2026-07-26) — **RESOLVED by P1.258, and its proposed fix rests on a mis-citation.** The symptom (null mandate RW + third-party flag yielding 15.0) is gone because the ×1.2 uplift no longer touches the Art. 132(2) fall-back. The proposed "clip at `CIU_FALLBACK_RW` per PS1/26 Art. 132(6)" is wrong: Art. 132 paragraph 6 is a **PRA notification provision**, not a cap on LTA/MBA RWEA. Do not re-file as a cap without a fresh citation.
